,
\Omega_{\rho}=\left\{X\in\mathbb{H}^{n+1}:d_{\mathbb{H}^{n+1}}(X,\Omega)\leq\rho\right\}
.

Theorem 6.3
.

The Steiner formulas for modified quermassintegrals of a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{H}^{n+1}

(n\geq 1)
are

\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{\rho})-\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega)=\sum_{i=k}^{n}C_{n-k}^{i-k}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}p_{i}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k-i)t}\sinh^{-k+i}tdt,\quad k=0,1,\ldots,n.

Proof.

By using (
2.16
), (
2.15
) and Lemma
5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega_{t}}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\det(A[\varphi_{t}])d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{t}^{-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda})d\sigma=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{t}^{-k}p_{n-k}(A[\varphi_{t}])d\sigma.

It follows that

(6.3)

C_{n}^{k}\frac{d}{dt}\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{t}^{-k}\sigma_{n-k}(A[\varphi_{t}])d\sigma=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}e^{-kt}\varphi^{-k}\sigma_{n-k}(A[\varphi_{t}])d\sigma.

By (
2.12
), we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{n-k}(A[\varphi_{t}(z)])=

\displaystyle\sigma_{n-k}\left((\varphi_{t})_{ij}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{|D\varphi_{t}|^{2}}{\varphi_{t}}\delta_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi_{t}-\frac{1}{\varphi_{t}}\right)\delta_{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{n-k}\left(e^{t}A_{ij}[\varphi]+\sinh t\frac{1}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}\right)

(6.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{n-k}C_{n-l}^{n-k-l}\varphi^{k+l-n}\sigma_{l}(A[\varphi])e^{lt}\sinh^{n-k-l}t.

Inserting (
6.4
) into (
6.3
) and using (
2.16
), (
2.15
), we have

\displaystyle C_{n}^{k}\frac{d}{dt}\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{n-k}C_{n-l}^{n-k-l}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{l-n}\sigma_{l}(A[\varphi])e^{(l-k)t}\sinh^{n-k-l}td\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{n-k}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\sigma_{n-l}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)C_{n-l}^{n-k-l}e^{(l-k)t}\sinh^{n-k-l}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\sigma_{i}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)C_{i}^{i-k}e^{(n-i-k)t}\sinh^{i-k}t.

Integrating both sides of the above formula from
0
to
\rho
, we have

\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{\rho})-\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega)=\sum_{i=k}^{n}C_{n-k}^{i-k}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}p_{i}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k-i)t}\sinh^{i-k}tdt.

This completes the proof of Theorem
6.3
.
∎

For the sake of completeness, let us check that the
k=0
case of Theorem
6.3
corresponds to the following well-known Steiner formula, see
[
71
, IV 18.4]
and
[
76
]
.

Corollary 6.1
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{H}^{n+1}

(n\geq 1)
be a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain. Then

\mathrm{Vol}(\Omega_{\rho})-\mathrm{Vol}(\Omega)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\sigma_{i}({\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}\cosh^{n-i}t\sinh^{i}tdt.

Proof.

A direct calculation yields

(6.5)

\sigma_{i}(\tilde{\kappa})=\sum_{s=0}^{i}(-1)^{i-s}C_{n}^{i}C_{i}^{s}p_{s}(\kappa)=\sum_{s=0}^{i}(-1)^{i-s}C_{n-s}^{i-s}\sigma_{s}(\kappa),\quad i=0,\ldots,n.

Taking
k=0
in Theorem
6.3
and using (
6.5
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}(\Omega_{\rho})-\mathrm{Vol}(\Omega)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\sigma_{i}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-i)t}\sinh^{i}tdt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{i}(-1)^{i-s}C_{n-s}^{i-s}\int_{\partial\Omega}\sigma_{s}(\kappa)d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-i)t}\sinh^{i}tdt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{n}\sum_{i=s}^{n}\left((-1)^{i-s}C_{n-s}^{i-s}\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-i)t}\sinh^{i}tdt\right)\int_{\partial\Omega}\sigma_{s}(\kappa)d\mu

(6.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=0}^{n}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n-s}(-1)^{n-j-s}C_{n-s}^{j}\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{jt}\sinh^{n-j}tdt\right)\int_{\partial\Omega}\sigma_{s}(\kappa)d\mu.

A direct calculation shows

(6.7)

\cosh^{n-s}t=(e^{t}-\sinh t)^{n-s}=\sum_{j=0}^{n-s}C_{n-s}^{j}e^{jt}(-1)^{n-s-j}\sinh^{n-s-j}t.

Inserting (
6.7
) into the right-hand side of (
6.6
), we obtain

\mathrm{Vol}(\Omega_{\rho})-\mathrm{Vol}(\Omega)=\sum_{s=0}^{n}\left(\int_{0}^{\rho}\cosh^{n-s}t\sinh^{s}tdt\right)\int_{\partial\Omega}\sigma_{s}(\kappa)d\mu.

We complete the proof of Corollary
6.1
.
∎

6.3.
Steiner formula for weighted volume in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}

We define the
weighted volume
of a domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
by
\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)=\int_{\Omega}\cosh rdv
. If the domain
\Omega
is smooth, then by the divergence theorem and (
3.18
), we have

(6.8)

\int_{\partial\Omega}\tilde{u}d\mu:=\int_{\partial\Omega}\langle V,\nu\rangle d\mu=\int_{\Omega}\mathrm{div}Vdv=(n+1)\int_{\Omega}\cosh rdv=(n+1)\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega).

Theorem 6.4
.

The weighted Steiner formula of a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{H}^{n+1}

(n\geq 1)
is

(6.9)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})-\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k+1)t}\sinh^{k}tdt

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k)t}\sinh^{k+1}tdt.

Equivalently, it holds that

\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})=\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)e^{(n+1)\rho}+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{k+1}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)e^{(n-k)\rho}\sinh^{k+1}\rho.

Proof.

Denote by
r_{t}
the radial function of
\Omega_{t}
defined on
\partial\Omega_{t}
. By the co-area formula and (
2.16
), we have

(6.10)

\frac{d}{dt}\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{t})=\int_{\partial\Omega_{t}}\cosh r_{t}d\mu_{t}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\cosh r_{t}\det(A[\varphi_{t}])d\sigma.

By
\varphi_{t}(z)=e^{t}\varphi(z)
and (
3.22
), we obtain

\displaystyle\cosh r_{t}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{|D\varphi_{t}|^{2}}{\varphi_{t}}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi_{t}+\frac{1}{\varphi_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{t}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi+\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)\right)-e^{t}\frac{1}{2\varphi}+e^{-t}\frac{1}{2\varphi}

(6.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{t}\cosh r-\sinh t\frac{1}{\varphi}.

Inserting (
6.11
) and (
6.4
) (take
k=0
in (
6.4
)) into (
6.10
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\left(e^{t}\cosh r-\sinh t\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{n}\frac{1}{\varphi^{n-l}}\sigma_{l}(A[\varphi])e^{lt}\sinh^{n-l}t\right)d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\cosh r\frac{1}{\varphi^{n-l}}\sigma_{l}(A[\varphi])d\sigma\right)e^{(l+1)t}\sinh^{n-l}t

(6.12)

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\frac{1}{\varphi^{n-l+1}}\sigma_{l}(A[\varphi])d\sigma\right)e^{lt}\sinh^{n-l+1}t.

Integrating the both sides of (
6.12
) from
0
to
\rho
yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})-\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{0})=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\cosh r\frac{1}{\varphi^{n-l}}\sigma_{l}(A[\varphi])d\sigma\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(l+1)t}\sinh^{n-l}tdt

(6.13)

\displaystyle-\sum_{l=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\frac{1}{\varphi^{n-l+1}}\sigma_{l}(A[\varphi])d\sigma\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{lt}\sinh^{n-l+1}tdt.

Taking
l=n-k
in (
6.13
), and using (
2.16
) and (
2.15
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})-\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k+1)t}\sinh^{k}tdt

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{\varphi}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k)t}\sinh^{k+1}tdt,

thus we obtain (
6.9
). Furthermore, by using (
3.24
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})-\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{\varphi}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k+1)t}\sinh^{k}tdt+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\tilde{u}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k+1)t}\sinh^{k}tdt

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{\varphi}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k)t}\sinh^{k+1}tdt

(6.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{\varphi}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k)t}\sinh^{k}t\cosh tdt+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\tilde{u}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k+1)t}\sinh^{k}tdt.

For
0\leq k\leq n
, by integration by parts, we have

(6.15)

\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k)t}\sinh^{k}t\cosh tdt=\frac{1}{k+1}e^{(n-k)\rho}\sinh^{k+1}\rho-\frac{n-k}{k+1}\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k)t}\sinh^{k+1}tdt.

By (
3.20
) and (
3.24
), we know

(6.16)

\int_{\partial\Omega}\tilde{u}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu=\frac{n-k+1}{k}\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{\varphi}\sigma_{k-1}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu,\quad k=1,\ldots,n.

Inserting (
6.15
) and (
6.16
) into (
6.14
), we have by (
3.24
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{k+1}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{\varphi}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)e^{(n-k)\rho}\sinh^{k+1}\rho

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{n-k}{k+1}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{\varphi}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k)t}\sinh^{k+1}tdt

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\frac{n-k}{k+1}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{1}{\varphi}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k)t}\sinh^{k+1}tdt+(n+1)\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n+1)t}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)e^{(n+1)\rho}+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{k+1}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)e^{(n-k)\rho}\sinh^{k+1}\rho.

∎

Remark 6.1
.

In Theorem
6.4
, the uniformly h-convex assumption of
\Omega
is not necessary, see Proposition
13.1
.

6.4.
Application

In
[
78
, Equation (25)]
, Chao Xia obtained the following geometric inequality for h-convex bounded domains,

(6.17)

\lim_{\rho\to+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})^{\frac{1}{n+1}}}{\sinh\rho}\leq\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}+\frac{1}{n+1}\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)^{-\frac{n}{n+1}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh rd\mu.

Combing (
6.17
) with the weighted Steiner formula (
6.9
), we prove the following new weighted Alexandrov-Fenchel type inequality for h-convex domains.

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
\Omega
be a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}

(n\geq 1)
. Then

\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{k+1}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{k}(\kappa)d\mu\leq\left(\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)^{\frac{1}{n+1}}+\frac{1}{n+1}\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)^{-\frac{n}{n+1}}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh rd\mu\right)^{n+1}.

Proof.

We will prove this theorem by calculating the left-hand side of (
6.17
) directly. Using the L’ Hospital’s rule, we have

\lim_{\rho\to+\infty}\frac{\int_{0}^{\rho}e^{(n-k+1)t}\sinh^{k}tdt}{\sinh^{n+1}\rho}=\lim_{\rho\to+\infty}\frac{e^{(n-k+1)\rho}\sinh^{k}\rho}{(n+1)\sinh^{n}\rho\cosh\rho}=\frac{2^{n-k+1}}{n+1}.

Dividing the both sides of (
6.9
) by
\sinh^{n+1}\rho
and taking
\rho\to+\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\to+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})}{\sinh^{n+1}\rho}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\frac{2^{n-k+1}}{n+1}-\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\frac{2^{n-k}}{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\frac{2^{n-k}}{n+1}+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\tilde{u}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\frac{2^{n-k}}{n+1}

(6.18)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2},

where
I_{1}
is the first term and
I_{2}
is the second term in the second line of (
6.18
). Using (
6.5
), we have

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{i}(\kappa)d\mu\right)\frac{(-1)^{k-i}C_{n-i}^{k-i}2^{n-k}}{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{i}(\kappa)d\mu\right)\left(\sum_{k=i}^{n}\frac{(-1)^{k-i}C_{n-i}^{k-i}2^{n-k}}{n+1}\right)

(6.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{i=0}^{n}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{i}(\kappa)d\mu.

By using (
3.19
), (
6.8
) and a similar calculation as above, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\tilde{u}\sigma_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)\frac{2^{n-k}}{n+1}=\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\int_{\partial\Omega}\tilde{u}\sigma_{k}(\kappa)d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)+\frac{1}{n+1}\frac{C_{n}^{k}}{C_{n}^{k-1}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{k-1}(\kappa)d\mu

(6.20)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\frac{n-k}{(n+1)(k+1)}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{k}(\kappa)d\mu.

Substituting (
6.19
) and (
6.20
) into the right-hand side of (
6.18
), we obtain

\lim_{\rho\to+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})}{\sinh^{n+1}\rho}=\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{k+1}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{k}(\kappa)d\mu.

Then Theorem
6.5
follows by substituting the above formula into (
6.17
).
∎

Here we give an explicit example of the result in Theorem
6.5
. Letting
n=2
and
\Omega\subset\mathbb{H}^{3}
be a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain, we have

\lim_{\rho\to+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega_{\rho})}{\sinh^{3}\rho}=\frac{1}{3}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{2}(\kappa)d\mu+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh rHd\mu+\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh rd\mu+\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega).

Then Theorem
6.5
reduces to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh r\sigma_{2}(\kappa)d\mu+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh rHd\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)^{-1}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh rd\mu\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{27}\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)^{-2}\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh rd\mu\right)^{3}.

For other Alexandrov-Fenchel type inequalities proved by using Steiner formulas in space forms, readers can refer to e.g.
[
66
,
77
]
.

7.
Existence of solutions to Horospherical
p
-Minkowski problem and Horospherical
p
-Christoffel-Minkowski problem

In Section
7
, we study Problem
5.1
and Problem
5.2
, i.e.

(7.1)

\varphi^{-p-k}(z)p_{n-k}\left(A[\varphi(z)]\right)=f(z),\quad k=0,1,\ldots,n-1,

where
u(z)
is the horospherical support function of the solution and
\varphi(z):=e^{u(z)}
. By (
2.15
), equation (
7.1
) is equivalent to

(7.2)

\varphi^{-p-n}(z)p_{n-k}\left(\tilde{\lambda}\left(G^{-1}(z)\right)\right)=f(z),\quad k=0,1,\ldots,n-1,

where
G
is the horospherical Gauss map of the solution, and
\tilde{\lambda}
are the shifted principal radii of curvature at
G^{-1}(z)
. When
n\geq 1
,
k=0
and
p=0
in equation (
7.1
),

(7.3)

p_{n}\left(A[\varphi(z)]\right)=f(z)

is the equation for the prescribed horospherical surface area measure problem by (
2.16
). When
n\geq 1
,
k=0
and
p=-n
in equation (
7.1
),

(7.4)

\varphi^{n}(z)p_{n}\left(A[\varphi(z)]\right)=f(z)

is the equation for prescribed shifted Gauss curvature problem.

To obtain the existence of solutions to the Christoffel-Minkowski problem and the
L_{p}
Christoffel-Minkowski problem in Euclidean space
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, researchers always need to make convex assumptions on the prescribed measures, see e.g.
[
40
,
42
,
19
]
. To obtain the existence of solutions to equation (
7.1
), we need to make the following assumptions on
f(z)
when
1\leq k\leq n-1
.

Assumption 7.1
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
1\leq k\leq n-1
be integers. Let
p\geq-n
be a real number and
f(z)
be a smooth positive function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
. For abbreviation, let
h(z):=f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(z)
.

(1)

If
p=-n
, then we assume that
h(z)
is a positive constant function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.

(2)

If
-n<p\leq-\frac{n+k}{2}
, then we assume that
h(z)
satisfies

D^{2}h-\left(\frac{n-3k-2p}{n-k}\right)|Dh|I+\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}\right)^{2}hI\geq 0.

(3)

If
-\frac{n+k}{2}<p<-k
, then we assume that
h(z)
satisfies

D^{2}h-\frac{(n-3k-2p)^{2}}{2(n+p)(n+k+2p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}I+\frac{1}{2}\frac{n+p}{n-k}hI\geq 0.

(4)

If
-k\leq p\leq n-2k
, then we assume that
h(z)
satisfies

D^{2}\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left|D\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)\right|^{2}}{h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}}I+\frac{1}{2}h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}I\geq 0.

(5)

If
p>n-2k
, then we assume that
h(z)
satisfies

D^{2}\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left|D\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)\right|^{2}}{h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}}I+\frac{n-k}{n+p}h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}I\geq 0.

We state the main results in Section
7
and their proofs will be given in Subsection
7.3
.

Theorem 7.2

(Horospherical
p
-Minkowski problem)
.

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer and
-\infty<p<+\infty
be a real number. Given a smooth positive even function
f(z)
defined on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
, the following equation has a smooth even and uniformly h-convex solution, i.e.

(7.5)

\varphi^{-p}(z)p_{n}\left(A[\varphi(z)]\right)=\gamma f(z)

for some
\gamma>0
.

Theorem 7.3

(Horospherical
p
-Christoffel-Minkowski problem)
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
1\leq k\leq n-1
be integers, and let
p\geq-n
be a real number. Given a smooth positive even function
f(z)
defined on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
that satisfies the Assumption
7.1
, the following equation has a smooth even and uniformly h-convex solution, i.e.

(7.6)

\varphi^{-p-k}p_{n-k}(A[\varphi(z)])=\gamma f(z)

for some
\gamma>0
.

Our following curvature flow (
7.7
) will be used to study the existence of solutions to equation (
7.1
). In the case that
n\geq 2
,
p=-n
and
f(z)
is a positive constant, the flow (
7.7
) was used to obtain a class of Alexandrov-Fenchel inequalities in hyperbolic space by Andrews et al.
[
3
, Corollary 1.9]
.

Let
n\geq 1
and
0\leq k\leq n-1
be integers, let
p
be a real number, and let
f(z)
be a smooth positive function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
. Suppose that
X:M\times[0,T)\to\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
is a family of smooth uniformly h-convex hypersurfaces, satisfying

(7.7)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial}{\partial t}X(x,t)=&\left(\Phi(t)\left(\cosh r-\widetilde{u}\right)^{\frac{n+p}{n-k}}(x,t)f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(z(x,t))-p_{n-k}^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))\right)\nu,\\
X(\cdot,0)=&M_{0}=\partial\Omega_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

Here
z(x,t)
is the horospherical Gauss map at
X(x,t)
of
M_{t}=X(M,t)=\partial\Omega_{t}
,
\tilde{\lambda}
are the shifted principal radii of curvature of
M_{t}
, and
\Phi(t)
is a scalar function to be specified immediately. Let
G_{t}:M_{t}\to\mathbb{S}^{n}
be the horospherical Gauss map of
M_{t}
and
u(z,t):=u_{\Omega_{t}}(z)
be the horospherical support function of
\Omega_{t}
, and
\varphi(z,t):=e^{u(z,t)}
. In this paper, the global term
\Phi(t)
is defined by

(7.8)

\Phi(t):=\frac{\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{-n}(z,t)p_{n-k}^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}\left(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t)\right)d\sigma}{\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(z)\varphi^{-n-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}(z,t)p_{n-k}\left(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t)\right)d\sigma},

where
\tilde{\lambda}(x,t)
are the shifted principal radii of curvature at
X(x,t):=G^{-1}_{t}(z)
of
M_{t}
. This choice of
\Phi(t)
ensures that
\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})
remains constant along the flow (
7.7
). We denote the speed function of flow (
7.7
) as

(7.9)

\mathscr{F}=\Phi(t)\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}(z,t)h(z)-F(\tilde{\kappa}(x,t)),

where
h(z):=f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(z)
,
F(\tilde{\kappa}):=\left(\frac{p_{n}(\tilde{\kappa})}{p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})}\right)^{\frac{1}{n-k}}
, and we used (
3.24
).

We will prove the following long time existence of the flow (
7.7
), and the proof will be given in Subsection
7.3
.

Theorem 7.4
.

Let
f(z)
be a smooth positive even function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
and
M_{0}=\partial\Omega_{0}
be a smooth, origin symmetric and uniformly h-convex hypersurface in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
.

(1)

If
k=0
,
-\infty<p<+\infty
and
n\geq 1
, then the flow (
7.7
) has a smooth uniformly h-convex solution
M_{t}
for all time
t>0
.

(2)

If
1\leq k\leq n-1
,
p\geq-n
,
n\geq 2
, and
f(z)
satisfies the Assumption
7.1
, then the flow (
7.7
) has a smooth uniformly h-convex solution
M_{t}
for all time
t>0
.

Remark 7.1
.

Let
n\geq 1
,
0\leq k\leq n-1
,
p\geq-n
, and
f(z)
be a positive constant. Let
M_{0}
be a smooth, origin symmetric and uniformly h-convex hypersurface in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
. In Theorem
8.2
, we will prove that flow (
7.7
) converges to a geodesic sphere centered at the origin.

The flow (
7.7
) is equivalent to a parabolic equation of
u(z,t)
by a similar argument as (
5.3
), i.e.

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}u(z,t)=

\displaystyle\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}-p_{n-k}^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda})

(7.10)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}-\varphi^{-1}p_{n-k}^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(A[\varphi]).

Then the scalar evolution equation of
\varphi(z,t)
is

(7.11)

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\varphi(z,t)=\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}-p_{n-k}^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(A[\varphi]).

If
\Omega
is an origin symmetric domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
, then
\varphi_{\Omega}(z)
is even on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
. Suppose that
f(z)
is a positive even function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
,
M_{0}
is a smooth, origin symmetric hypersurface, and
\{M_{t}\}
is a solution to the flow (
7.7
) for
t\in[0,T)
, then (
7.11
) implies that
M_{t}
is origin symmetric.

To avoid confusion, when we do calculations on a uniformly h-convex hypersurface
M=\partial\Omega
, we view
z
as a vector field defined on
M
by
z=G_{\Omega}(X)
for
X\in M
. Similarly, we view
X=G_{\Omega}^{-1}(z)
when we do calculations on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.

7.1.
Monotone quantities

Let
p
be a real number and
f(z)
be a smooth positive function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
. Let
\Omega
be a h-convex domain with horospherical support function
u(z)
, and
\varphi(z):=e^{u(z)}
. We define

(7.12)

J_{p}(\Omega):=\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{p}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f(z)\varphi^{p}(z)d\sigma,\quad p\neq 0,\\
\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f(z)u(z)d\sigma,\quad p=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
M_{t}=\partial\Omega_{t}
be a smooth uniformly h-convex solution to the flow (
7.7
). Then

(7.13)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})=

\displaystyle 0,\quad\forall\ t\in[0,T),

(7.14)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}J_{p}(\Omega_{t})\leq

\displaystyle 0,\quad\forall\ t\in[0,T).

Moreover, equality holds in (
7.14
) if and only if
f^{-1}(z)\varphi^{-p-n}(z,t)p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))
is constant.

Proof.

From Lemma
5.1
, we have by (
2.16
) and (
2.15
) that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})=

\displaystyle\int_{M_{t}}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{-n}\frac{p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda})}{p_{n}(\tilde{\lambda})}p_{n}(\tilde{\lambda})\mathscr{F}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda})\mathscr{F}d\sigma.

Substituting (
7.9
) into the right-hand side and using (
7.8
), we obtain

\frac{d}{dt}\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})=\Phi(t)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}\varphi^{-n-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda})d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{-n}p_{n-k}^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda})d\sigma=0.

When
p\neq 0
, we have from (
7.10
) that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}J_{p}(\Omega_{t})=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{1}{p}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f(z)\varphi^{p}(z,t)d\sigma\right)=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f\varphi^{p}\mathscr{F}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi(t)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}\varphi^{p-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f\varphi^{p}p_{n-k}^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda})d\sigma.

When
p=0
, we have by using (
7.10
)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}J_{0}(\Omega_{t})=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f(z)u(z,t)d\sigma\right)=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f\mathscr{F}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi(t)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}\varphi^{-\frac{n}{n-k}}d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}fp_{n-k}^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda})d\sigma.

By the definition of
\Phi(t)
in (
7.8
), it suffices to prove the following inequality for all
p\in(-\infty,+\infty)
,

(7.15)

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}\varphi^{p-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}d\sigma\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{-n}p_{n-k}^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda})d\sigma\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f\varphi^{p}p_{n-k}^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda})d\sigma\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}\varphi^{-n-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda})d\sigma.

In the case
k=n-1
, the inequality (
7.15
) follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the equality holds if and only if

f^{-1}(z)\varphi^{-n-p}(z,t)p_{1}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))

is constant on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
. In the case
0\leq k\leq n-2
, we let
dm(z,t)=f^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}(z)\varphi^{p-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}(z,t)d\sigma(z)
and
\eta(z,t)=f^{\frac{1}{n-k}-1}(z)\varphi^{\frac{n+p}{n-k}-n-p}(z,t)p_{n-k}^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))
. Then (
7.15
) becomes

(7.16)

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}dm\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\eta dm-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\eta^{-\frac{1}{n-k-1}}dm\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\eta^{\frac{n-k}{n-k-1}}dm\leq 0.

The above inequality (
7.16
) follows from

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\left(\eta^{-\frac{1}{n-k-1}}(z_{1},t)-\eta^{-\frac{1}{n-k-1}}(z_{2},t)\right)\left(\eta^{\frac{n-k}{n-k-1}}(z_{1},t)-\eta^{\frac{n-k}{n-k-1}}(z_{2},t)\right)dm(z_{1},t)dm(z_{2},t)\leq 0,

with equality if and only if
\eta(z,t)
is constant on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
, i.e.

f^{-1}(z)\varphi^{-n-p}(z,t)p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))

is constant. Hence we complete the proof of Lemma
7.1
.
∎

7.2.
A priori estimates

In order to achieve the long time existence of flow (
7.7
), we need to do the related a priori estimates.

7.2.1.
C^{0}
estimate

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
M_{t}=\partial\Omega
be a smooth, origin symmetric and uniformly h-convex solution to the flow (
7.7
). Let

r_{\min}(t):=\min_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n}}r(\theta,t),\quad r_{\max}(t):=\max_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n}}r(\theta,t),

and

u_{\min}(t):=\min_{z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}}u(z,t),\quad u_{\max}(t):=\max_{z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}}u(z,t).

Then along the flow (
7.7
), there exist two positive constants
c
,
C
such that

(7.17)

c\leq r_{\min}(t)=u_{\min}(t)\leq u_{\max}(t)=r_{\max}(t)\leq C,\quad\forall\ t\in[0,T),

where
c
and
C
only depend on
n
,
k
and
M_{0}
.

Proof.

In this proof, we fix a time
t\in[0,T)
. By (
7.13
), we have

\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})=\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{0}).

Now we choose
z_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
such that
u(z_{0},t)=u_{\min}(t)
. Since
\Omega_{t}
is origin symmetric, we know
\Omega_{t}\subset\overline{B}_{z_{0}}(u(z_{0},t))\cap\overline{B}_{-z_{0}}(u(z_{0},t))
. When
u(z_{0},t)
goes to
0
, we have that
\widetilde{W}_{k}\left(\overline{B}_{z_{0}}(u(z_{0},t))\cap\overline{B}_{-z_{0}}(u(z_{0},t))\right)
also goes to
0
by Proposition
5.1
. Hence

(7.18)

u_{\min}(t)\geq c

for some constant
c
along the flow (
7.7
). Since

\Omega_{t}=\bigcap_{z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}}\overline{B}_{z}(u(z,t)),

we have
B(u_{\min}(t))\subset\Omega_{t}
. Then

\widetilde{W}_{k}\left(B(u_{\min}(t))\right)\leq\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})=\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{0}).

Consequently,
u_{\min}(t)\leq C
for some constant
C
along the flow (
7.7
). Now we let
z_{1}\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
such that
u(z_{1},t)=u_{\max}(t)
and set
\varphi_{\max}(t)=e^{u_{\max}(t)}
. Let
\overline{X}=X(z_{1},t)
as in (
2.9
). Since the horo-ball
\overline{B}_{z}(r)
is given by

\log(-\langle X,(z,1)\rangle)\leq r,

we have

-\langle\overline{X},(z,1)\rangle\leq\varphi(z,t),\quad\forall\ z\in\mathbb{S}^{n},

which means
\overline{X}\in\overline{B}_{z}(u(z))
. By (
2.9
), we have

(7.19)

\frac{1}{2}\varphi_{\max}(t)(1+\langle z_{1},z\rangle)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{|D\varphi(z_{1},t)|^{2}}{\varphi_{\max}(t)}+\frac{1}{\varphi_{\max}(t)}\right)(1-\langle z_{1},z\rangle)+\langle D\varphi(z_{1},t),z\rangle\leq\varphi(z,t)

for any
z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
. Now we set

E=\{z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}:\langle z_{1},z\rangle\geq 0\}.

Then by
D\varphi(z_{1},t)=0
and
\varphi_{\max}(t)\geq e^{u_{\min}(t)}>1
, we have
\varphi(z,t)\geq\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi_{\max}(t)+\frac{1}{\varphi_{\max}(t)}\right)=\cosh u_{\max}(t)
for
z\in E
. Since
\Omega_{t}
is origin symmetric, we can assume
z_{0}\in E
without loss of generality. Then we have

\cosh u_{\max}(t)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi_{\max}(t)+\frac{1}{\varphi_{\max}(t)}\right)\leq e^{u_{\min}(t)}\leq C,

which means

(7.20)

u_{\max}(t)\leq C

for some constant
C
. Finally, Lemma
7.2
follows from the estimates in (
7.18
), (
7.20
) and formula (
3.46
).
∎

7.2.2.
C^{1}
estimates

Lemma 7.3
.

For a smooth, origin symmetric and uniformly h-convex hypersurface
M=\partial\Omega
in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
, we have

(7.21)

|D\varphi(z)|<\varphi(z),\quad\forall\ z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}.

Proof.

Fix a point
z_{0}\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
such that
\min_{z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi(z)=\varphi(z_{0})
. Since
\Omega\subset\overline{B}_{z_{0}}(u(z_{0}))\cap\overline{B}_{-z_{0}}(u(z_{0}))
, inequality (
3.47
) implies

\cosh r(\theta)\leq\varphi(z_{0}),\quad\forall\ \theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n}.

For any
z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
, by using (
3.22
) and
\varphi(z)\geq\varphi(z_{0})
, we have

\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi^{2}}(z)=\frac{2(\cosh r-\cosh u(z))}{\varphi(z)}\leq\frac{2\sinh u(z_{0})}{\varphi(z_{0})}=1-\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}(z_{0})}<1.

Hence, inequality
|D\varphi(z)|<\varphi(z)
holds for all
z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
.
∎

Corollary 7.1
.

Let
M_{t}=\partial\Omega
be a smooth, origin symmetric and uniformly h-convex solution to the flow (
7.7
). Then there is a positive constant
C
depending only on
n
,
k
and
M_{0}
such that

(7.22)

|D\varphi(z,t)|\leq C,\quad\forall\ (z,t)\in\mathbb{S}^{n}\times[0,T).

Proof.

Using (
7.17
) and (
7.21
), we have
|D\varphi(z,t)|<\varphi(z,t)\leq C
along the flow, which induces Corollary
7.1
.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
M_{t}=\partial\Omega
be a smooth, origin symmetric and uniformly h-convex solution to the flow (
7.7
). Along the flow (
7.7
), it holds that

(7.23)

\Phi(t)\leq C,\quad\forall\ t\in[0,T),

where
C
is a positive constant that only depends on
n
,
k
,
p
,
||f(z)||_{C^{0}(\mathbb{S}^{n})}
and
M_{0}
. Furthermore, if we assume
\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\left(\tilde{\kappa}_{i}/\tilde{\kappa}_{j}\right)\leq C^{\prime}
on
M\times[0,T)
along the flow for some positive constant
C^{\prime}
, then

(7.24)

\Phi(t)\geq c,\quad\forall\ t\in[0,T)

for some positive constant
c
that only depends on
C^{\prime}
,
n
,
k
,
p
,
\min_{z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}}f(z)
and
M_{0}
.

Proof.

We say
a\sim b
if and only if
a\leq Cb
,
b\leq Ca
for some constant
C>0
depending only on
n
,
k
and
M_{0}
. Combining the a priori estimates in (
7.17
) and (
7.22
) with formulas (
3.24
) and (
3.23
), we have

(7.25)

\displaystyle\cosh r(x,t)-\tilde{u}(x,t)=\frac{1}{\varphi(z,t)}\sim 1,

(7.26)

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(x,t)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi}(z,t)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi(z,t)-\frac{1}{\varphi(z,t)}\right)\sim 1

along the flow (
7.7
). By (
2.15
) and (
2.16
), we have

\int_{M_{t}}\varphi^{n}(z,t)p_{n}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}d\sigma=\omega_{n}
, which induces
\int_{M_{t}}p_{n}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}\sim 1
. Using (
7.25
), (
7.26
) and (
3.20
), we know
\int_{M_{t}}p_{m}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}\sim\int_{M_{t}}p_{m+1}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}
for
m=0,1,\ldots,n-1
. Hence we have

(7.27)

\int_{M_{t}}p_{m}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}\sim 1,\quad m=0,1,\ldots,n

along the flow. Using (
2.16
), (
2.15
) and (
7.25
) in (
7.27
), we get

\displaystyle 1\sim

\displaystyle\int_{M_{t}}p_{m}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}p_{m}(\tilde{\kappa})\det A[\varphi]d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}p_{m}(\tilde{\kappa})\varphi^{-n}p_{n}^{-1}(\tilde{\kappa})d\sigma=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}p_{n-m}(\tilde{\lambda})\varphi^{-n}d\sigma

(7.28)

\displaystyle\sim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}p_{n-m}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))d\sigma,\quad m=0,1,\ldots,n,

where
X(x,t)=G_{t}^{-1}(z)
. Formulas (
7.25
) and (
7.28
) imply

(7.29)

\frac{1}{C_{1}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(z)\varphi^{-n-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}(z,t)p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))d\sigma\leq C_{2}

along the flow (
7.7
) for some constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
. Here
C_{1}
only depends on
n
,
k
,
p
,
||f(z)||_{C^{0}(\mathbb{S}^{n})}
and
M_{0}
, and
C_{2}
only depends on
n
,
k
,
p
,
\min_{z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}}f(z)
and
M_{0}
. If
n\geq 2
and
k\leq n-2
, then (
3.6
) yields

(7.30)

p_{n-k}^{\frac{n-k-1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))\leq p_{n-k-1}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t)).

When
n\geq 1
and
k=n-1
, we note that (
7.30
) is trivial. Using (
7.30
), (
7.25
) and (
7.28
), we have

(7.31)

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{-n}(z,t)p_{n-k}^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))d\sigma\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{-n}(z,t)p_{n-k-1}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))d\sigma\leq C

along the flow (
7.7
) for some constant
C
. Combining (
7.29
), (
7.31
) with (
7.8
), we obtain (
7.23
).

Now we estimate
\Phi(t)
from below. For convenience, we assume
\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(x,t)\leq\cdots\leq\tilde{\kappa}_{n}(x,t)
at
X(x,t)\in M_{t}
, which means
\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(x,t)\geq\cdots\geq\tilde{\lambda}_{n}(x,t)
. By the assumption in Lemma
7.4
, we have

(7.32)

p_{n-k}^{\frac{n-k-1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))\geq\left(\tilde{\lambda}_{n}(x,t)\right)^{n-k-1}\geq\left(\frac{1}{C^{\prime}}\tilde{\lambda}_{1}(x,t)\right)^{n-k-1}\geq\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{-(n-k-1)}p_{n-k-1}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t)).

Then by using (
7.31
), (
7.32
), (
7.25
) and (
7.28
), there exists a large positive constant
C
such that

\displaystyle C\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{-n}(z,t)p_{n-k}^{1-\frac{1}{n-k}}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))d\sigma

(7.33)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(C^{\prime}\right)^{-(n-k-1)}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi^{-n}(z,t)p_{n-k-1}(\tilde{\lambda}(x,t))d\sigma\geq\frac{1}{C}.

Then (
7.24
) follows from (
7.29
), (
7.33
) and the definition of
\Phi(t)
in (
7.8
). This completes the proof of Lemma
7.4
.
∎

7.2.3.
Pinching estimates

Along a general flow

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}X=\mathscr{F}\nu,

the following evolution equations for hypersurfaces in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
are well known, see e.g.
[
3
, Equations (2.7) and (2,9)]
,

(7.34)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}g_{ij}=

\displaystyle 2\mathscr{F}h_{ij},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}h_{i}{}^{j}=

\displaystyle-\nabla^{j}\nabla_{i}\mathscr{F}-\mathscr{F}(h_{i}{}^{l}h_{l}{}^{j}-\delta_{i}{}^{j}).

Hence

(7.35)

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}=-\nabla^{j}\nabla_{i}\mathscr{F}-\mathscr{F}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{j}-2\mathscr{F}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j},

where
\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}=h_{i}{}^{j}-\delta_{i}{}^{j}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Along the flow (
7.7
), the shifted Weingarten matrix
\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}
evolves as

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}=

\displaystyle\dot{F}^{lm}\nabla_{m}\nabla_{l}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}+\ddot{F}^{lm,qs}\nabla_{i}\tilde{h}_{lm}\nabla^{j}\tilde{h}_{qs}+\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}\delta_{i}{}^{j}

\displaystyle+\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}-\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{q}{}^{j}-F\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{j}

(7.36)

\displaystyle-\mathscr{F}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{j}-2\mathscr{F}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}-\Phi\nabla^{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right).

Proof.

Recall that
F\left(\tilde{\kappa}\right):=\left(\frac{p_{n}(\tilde{\kappa})}{p_{k}\left(\tilde{\kappa}\right)}\right)^{\frac{1}{n-k}}
. By the Simons’ identity (see e.g.,
[
6
, Equation (2-7)]
,
[
45
, Equation (3.5)]
), we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{j}\nabla_{i}F=

\displaystyle\nabla^{j}(\dot{F}^{lm}\nabla_{i}\tilde{h}_{lm})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dot{F}^{lm}\nabla^{j}\nabla_{i}\tilde{h}_{lm}+\ddot{F}^{lm,qs}\nabla_{i}\tilde{h}_{lm}\nabla^{j}\tilde{h}_{qs}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dot{F}^{lm}\nabla_{m}\nabla_{l}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}+\dot{F}^{lm}((h^{2})_{lm}+g_{lm})h_{i}{}^{j}-\dot{F}^{lm}h_{lm}((h^{2})_{i}{}^{j}+\delta_{i}{}^{j})

\displaystyle+\ddot{F}^{lm,qs}\nabla_{i}\tilde{h}_{lm}\nabla^{j}\tilde{h}_{qs}.

This together with (
3.15
) gives

\displaystyle\nabla^{j}\nabla_{i}F=

\displaystyle\dot{F}^{lm}\nabla_{m}\nabla_{l}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}+\dot{F}^{lm}\left((\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}+\delta_{l}{}^{q})(\tilde{h}_{qm}+g_{qm})+g_{lm}\right)\left(\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}+\delta_{i}{}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle-\dot{F}^{lm}\left(\tilde{h}_{lm}+g_{lm}\right)\left((\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{q}+\delta_{i}{}^{q})(\tilde{h}_{q}{}^{j}+\delta_{q}{}^{j})+\delta_{i}{}^{j}\right)+\ddot{F}^{lm,qs}\nabla_{i}\tilde{h}_{lm}\nabla^{j}\tilde{h}_{qs}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dot{F}^{lm}\nabla_{m}\nabla_{l}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}+\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}+\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}\delta_{i}{}^{j}-\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{q}{}^{j}

(7.37)

\displaystyle-F\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{j}+\ddot{F}^{lm,qs}\nabla_{i}\tilde{h}_{lm}\nabla^{j}\tilde{h}_{qs}.

By (
7.35
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}=

\displaystyle-\nabla^{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h-F\right)-\mathscr{F}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{j}-2\mathscr{F}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}

(7.38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla^{j}\nabla_{i}F-\Phi\nabla^{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right)-\mathscr{F}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{j}-2\mathscr{F}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}.

Then we obtain (
7.36
) by inserting (
7.37
) into (
7.38
). This completes the proof of Lemma
7.5
.
∎

The following Lemma
7.6
shows how Assumption
7.1
works in the pinching estimates, i.e. Proposition
7.1
below.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
1\leq k\leq n-1
be integers, and
p>-n
be a real number. Let
f(z)
be a smooth positive function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
satisfying Assumption
7.1
. Suppose that
M=\partial\Omega
is a smooth uniformly h-convex hypersurface in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
with horospherical support function
u(z)
. Let
\varphi(z)=e^{u(z)}
and
h(z)=f^{-\frac{1}{n-k}}(z)
. Let
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\}
be a local orthonormal frame for
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.

(1)

If
-n<p\leq-\frac{n+k}{2}
, and in addition that
\Omega
is origin symmetric, then

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle-\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}

(7.39)

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}h\geq 0,\quad\forall 1\leq i\leq n,

on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.

(2)

If
-\frac{n+k}{2}<p<-k
, then (
7.39
) holds on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.

(3)

If
-k\leq p\leq n-2k
, then (
7.39
) holds on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.

(4)

If
p>n-2k
, and in addition that
\Omega
contains the origin, then (
7.39
) holds on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.

Proof.

If
-n<p<-k
, then
n+p>0
,
k+p<0
, and
n-2k-p>n-k>0
. Hence

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle-\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}h

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\varphi|Dh|\cdot|D\varphi|+\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi|Dh|\cdot|D\varphi|+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h|D\varphi|^{2}

(7.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\mathcal{A}\varphi|D\varphi|\cdot|Dh|+\mathcal{B}|D\varphi|^{2}h+\mathcal{C}\varphi^{2}h

holds for all
-n<p<-k
, where

\mathcal{A}:=1-\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}>0,\quad\mathcal{B}:=\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-\frac{1}{2}\right),\quad\mathcal{C}:=\frac{1}{2}\frac{n+p}{n-k}.

Now we divide the remaining proof of Lemma
7.6
into four cases.

Case 1.

-n<p\leq-\frac{n+k}{2}
, and
\Omega
is in addition origin symmetric.
In this case, we have
\mathcal{B}\leq 0
. As we assumed
\Omega
is origin symmetric, the estimate (
7.21
) and Assumption
7.1
imply that

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h-\mathcal{A}\varphi|D\varphi|\cdot|Dh|I+\mathcal{B}|D\varphi|^{2}hI+\mathcal{C}\varphi^{2}hI

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h-\mathcal{A}\varphi^{2}|Dh|I+\mathcal{B}\varphi^{2}hI+\mathcal{C}\varphi^{2}hI

(7.41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}\left(D^{2}h-\left(\frac{n-3k-2p}{n-k}\right)|Dh|I+\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}\right)^{2}hI\right)\geq 0.

Combining (
7.40
) with (
7.41
), (
7.39
) is proved for Case 1.

Case 2.

-\frac{n+k}{2}<p<-k
.
It is direct to see that
\mathcal{B}>0
in Case 2. By the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality, we have

\mathcal{A}\varphi|D\varphi|\cdot|Dh|\leq\mathcal{B}|D\varphi|^{2}h+\frac{\mathcal{A}^{2}}{4\mathcal{B}}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}\varphi^{2}.

This together with Assumption
7.1
implies that

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h-\mathcal{A}\varphi|D\varphi|\cdot|Dh|I+\mathcal{B}|D\varphi|^{2}hI+\mathcal{C}\varphi^{2}hI

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}\left(D^{2}h-\frac{\mathcal{A}^{2}}{4\mathcal{B}}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}I+\mathcal{C}hI\right)

(7.42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}\left(D^{2}h-\frac{(n-3k-2p)^{2}}{2(n+p)(n+k+2p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}I+\frac{1}{2}\frac{n+p}{n-k}hI\right)\geq 0.

Combining (
7.40
) with (
7.42
), we obtain (
7.39
) for Case 2.

Case 3.

-k\leq p\leq n-2k
.
Using
p\geq-k
and the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\varphi^{2}\frac{\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle^{2}}{h},

\displaystyle-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}-\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\varphi^{2}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}.

Using the above inequalities, we obtain

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle-\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}h

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\varphi^{2}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\varphi^{2}\frac{\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle^{2}}{h}+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}h

(7.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}\left(D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\frac{\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle^{2}}{h}-\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}h\right).

Applying the assumption
p\leq n-2k
to (
7.43
), we have

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle-\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}h

(7.44)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}\left(D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\frac{\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle^{2}}{h}-\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h\right).

On the other hand, a direct calculation yields

\displaystyle D_{i}\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)=\frac{n-k}{n+p}h^{-\frac{k+p}{n+p}}D_{i}h,\quad D_{i}D_{i}\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)=\frac{n-k}{n+p}h^{-\frac{k+p}{n+p}}\left(D_{i}D_{i}h-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\frac{\left(D_{i}h\right)^{2}}{h}\right),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left|D\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)\right|^{2}}{h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}}=\frac{1}{2}h^{-\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\left(\frac{n-k}{n+p}\right)^{2}h^{-\frac{2(k+p)}{n+p}}|Dh|^{2}=\frac{n-k}{n+p}h^{-\frac{k+p}{n+p}}\left(\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}\right),

and hence

\displaystyle D_{i}D_{i}\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left|D\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)\right|^{2}}{h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}}+\frac{1}{2}h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n-k}{n+p}h^{-\frac{k+p}{n+p}}\left(D_{i}D_{i}h-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\frac{\left(D_{i}h\right)^{2}}{h}\right)-\frac{n-k}{n+p}h^{-\frac{k+p}{n+p}}\left(\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}\right)+\frac{1}{2}h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}

(7.45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n-k}{n+p}h^{-\frac{k+p}{n+p}}\left(D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\frac{\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle^{2}}{h}-\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h\right).

Combining (
7.44
) with (
7.45
) and using Assumption
7.1
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle-\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}h

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{2}h^{\frac{k+p}{n+p}}\left(D_{i}D_{i}\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left|D\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)\right|^{2}}{h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}}+\frac{1}{2}h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0.

Thus we obtain (
7.39
) for Case 3.

Case 4.

p>n-2k
, and
\Omega
contains the origin in addition. In this case, we know
\varphi(z)=e^{u(z)}\geq 1
and hence

\frac{1}{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n+p)}\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n+p)}=\frac{n-k}{n+p}.

Using (
7.43
), (
7.45
) and Assumption
7.1
, we have

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle-\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}h

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}\left(D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\frac{\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle^{2}}{h}-\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}h\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}\left(D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\frac{\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle^{2}}{h}-\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}+\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n+p)}\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}\right)h\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}\left(D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\frac{k+p}{n+p}\frac{\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle^{2}}{h}-\frac{n-k}{2(n+p)}\frac{|Dh|^{2}}{h}\right)+\varphi^{2}h

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{2}h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\left(D_{i}D_{i}\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left|D\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)\right|^{2}}{h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}}\right)+\varphi^{2}h

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{2}h^{\frac{k+p}{n+p}}\left(D_{i}D_{i}\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\left|D\left(h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)\right|^{2}}{h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}}+\frac{n-k}{n+p}h^{\frac{n-k}{n+p}}\right)

(7.46)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0.

Thus we obtain (
7.39
) for Case 4.

We complete the proof of Lemma
7.6
.
∎

Now we recall the tensor maximum principle proved by Andrews
[
8
, Theorem 3.2]
.

Theorem 7.5

(
[
8
]
)
.

Let
S_{ij}
be a smooth time-varying symmetric tensor field on a compact manifold
M
satisfying

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}S_{ij}=a^{kl}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{l}S_{ij}+b^{k}\nabla_{k}S_{ij}+N_{ij},

where
a^{kl}
and
b^{k}
are smooth,
\nabla
is a (possibly time-dependent) smooth symmetric connection, and
a^{kl}
is positive definite everywhere. Suppose that

N_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}+\sup_{\Gamma}2a^{kl}\left(2\Gamma_{k}{}^{p}\nabla_{l}S_{ip}v^{i}-\Gamma_{k}{}^{p}\Gamma_{l}{}^{q}S_{pq}\right)\geq 0,

whenever
S_{ij}\geq 0
and
S_{ij}v^{j}=0
and
\Gamma
is an
n\times n
-matrix. If
S_{ij}
is positive definite everywhere on
M
at
t=0
and on
\partial M
for
0\leq t\leq T
, then it is positive on
M\times[0,T]
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
n\geq 2
be an integer. Suppose that one of the following assumptions of
k
,
p
,
f(z)
, and
M_{t}
holds:

•

1\leq k\leq n-1
,
p>-n
,
f(z)
is a smooth positive even function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
satisfying Assumption
7.1
, and
M_{t}=\partial\Omega_{t}
is a smooth, origin symmetric uniformly h-convex solution to the flow (
7.7
);

•

0\leq k\leq n-1
,
p=-n
,
f(z)
is a positive constant function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
, and
M_{t}
is a smooth uniformly h-convex solution to the flow (
7.7
);

•

k=0
,
-\infty<p<+\infty
,
f(z)
is a smooth positive even function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
, and
M_{t}
is a smooth, origin symmetric uniformly h-convex solution to the flow (
7.7
).

Then there exists a positive constant
\varepsilon_{0}
depending only on
M_{0}
,
k
,
p
and
||f(z)||_{C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{n})}
, such that along the flow (
7.7
),

\tilde{h}_{ij}\geq\varepsilon_{0}\tilde{H}g_{ij},

where
\tilde{H}=g^{lm}\tilde{h}_{lm}
is the shifted mean curvature. Therefore, along the flow (
7.7
),

(7.47)

\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{j}}(x,t)\leq C(\varepsilon_{0}),\quad\forall\ (x,t)\in M\times[0,T),

where
\tilde{\kappa}_{i}=\kappa_{i}-1
,
1\leq i\leq n
, are the shifted principal curvatures, and
C(\varepsilon_{0})=\left(1-(n-1)\varepsilon_{0}\right)/\varepsilon_{0}
is a positive constant.

Proof.

Let
S_{ij}=\tilde{h}_{ij}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}g_{ij}
. By (
7.34
), we have

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}S_{ij}=\partial_{t}\left(\left(\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}\delta_{i}{}^{l}\right)g_{lj}\right)=\left(\partial_{t}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}-\varepsilon\partial_{t}\tilde{H}\delta_{i}{}^{l}\right)g_{lj}+2\mathscr{F}h_{j}{}^{l}S_{il}.

We set a parabolic operator
\mathcal{L}=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}-\dot{F}^{lm}\nabla_{m}\nabla_{l}
. Using (
7.36
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}S_{ij}=

\displaystyle\ddot{F}^{lm,qs}\nabla_{i}\tilde{h}_{lm}\nabla_{j}\tilde{h}_{qs}-\varepsilon\ddot{F}^{lm,qs}\nabla^{\iota}\tilde{h}_{lm}\nabla_{\iota}\tilde{h}_{qs}g_{ij}

\displaystyle+\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}(1-\varepsilon n)g_{ij}+\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}S_{ij}-\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}(\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qj}-\varepsilon|\tilde{A}|^{2}g_{ij})

\displaystyle-F(\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{lj}-\varepsilon|\tilde{A}|^{2}g_{ij})-\mathscr{F}(\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{lj}-\varepsilon|\tilde{A}|^{2}g_{ij})-2\mathscr{F}S_{ij}

\displaystyle-\Phi\left(\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right)-\varepsilon\Delta\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right)g_{ij}\right)+2\mathscr{F}h_{j}{}^{l}S_{il}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle N_{ij}+\tilde{N}_{ij},

where
|\tilde{A}|^{2}:=\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}\tilde{h}_{j}{}^{i}
and

\tilde{N}_{ij}=\ddot{F}^{lm,qs}\nabla_{i}\tilde{h}_{lm}\nabla_{j}\tilde{h}_{qs}-\varepsilon\ddot{F}^{lm,qs}\nabla^{\iota}\tilde{h}_{lm}\nabla_{\iota}\tilde{h}_{qs}g_{ij}.

According to Theorem
7.5
, we only need to show

N_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}+\tilde{N}_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}+2\sup_{\Gamma}\dot{F}^{lm}\left(2\Gamma_{l}{}^{q}\nabla_{m}S_{iq}v^{i}-\Gamma_{l}{}^{q}\Gamma_{m}{}^{s}S_{qs}\right)\geq 0,

whenever
S_{ij}\geq 0
and
S_{ij}v^{j}=0
,
|v|=1
. Let
(x_{0},t_{0})
be the point where
S_{ij}
has a null vector
v=v^{i}\partial_{i}X
.

By Lemma
3.4
and (
3.10
),
F=\left(\frac{p_{n}}{p_{k}}\right)^{\frac{1}{n-k}}
is concave and inverse-concave on the positive cone
\Gamma^{+}
. Recall that
\nabla_{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}
is a Codazzi tensor. Hence
F(\tilde{\kappa})
satisfies all the assumptions to apply
[
8
, Theorem 4.1]
. Consequently, we only need to prove
N_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}\geq 0
at
(x_{0},t_{0})
.

Remark that
S_{ij}v^{j}=0
is equivalent to
\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}v_{l}=\varepsilon\tilde{H}v_{i}
, where
v_{i}=v^{j}g_{ij}
. Then by the definition of
N_{ij}
, we have

\displaystyle N_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}=

\displaystyle\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}(1-\varepsilon n)+\varepsilon(\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}+F+\mathscr{F})(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})

\displaystyle-\Phi\left(\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right)-\varepsilon\Delta\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right)g_{ij}\right)v^{i}v^{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}(1-\varepsilon n)+\varepsilon\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})+\varepsilon\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})

(7.48)

\displaystyle-\Phi\left(\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right)-\varepsilon\Delta\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right)g_{ij}\right)v^{i}v^{j}.

For the last two terms in the right-hand side of (
7.48
), we have

(7.49)

\displaystyle-\Phi\left(\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right)-\varepsilon\Delta\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\right)g_{ij}\right)v^{i}v^{j}=\left(I\right)+\left(II\right)+\left(III\right),

where

(7.50)

\displaystyle\left(I\right):=

\displaystyle-\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\left(\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}h-\varepsilon\Delta hg_{ij}\right)v^{i}v^{j},

(7.51)

\displaystyle\left(II\right):=

\displaystyle-2\Phi\left(\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)\nabla_{j}h-\varepsilon\langle\nabla\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right),\nabla h\rangle g_{ij}\right)v^{i}v^{j},

(7.52)

\displaystyle\left(III\right):=

\displaystyle-\Phi h\left(\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)-\varepsilon\Delta\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)g_{ij}\right)v^{i}v^{j}.

Now we parameterize
M_{t_{0}}
around
X(x_{0},t_{0})
by normal coordinates for
\mathbb{S}^{n}
about
z_{0}=G_{t_{0}}(X(x_{0},t_{0}))
, such that
A_{ij}[\varphi(z_{0},t_{0})]
is diagonal, and the corresponding coordinate vector fields on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
are
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\}
. By the above convention, we have

(7.53)

\left(dG_{t_{0}}\right)_{X(x,t_{0})}\left(\partial_{i}X\right)=e_{i},\quad 1\leq i\leq n.

We write
\nabla_{i}
for
\nabla_{\partial_{i}X}
on
M_{t_{0}}
and
D_{i}
for
D_{e_{i}}
on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
. The calculations of (
7.49
) are divided into three parts.

Part 1
. In this part, we calculate (
7.50
). Recall that
\widetilde{\nabla}
denotes the connection of
\left(\mathbb{R}^{n+1,1},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\right)
. By identifying
\mathbb{S}^{n}
with
\mathbb{S}^{n}\times\{1\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n+1,1}
, we extend the definition of
h(z)
from
\mathbb{S}^{n}
to
\mathbb{R}^{n+1,1}\backslash\left(\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}\right)
by
h((y,y_{n+1})):=h\left(\frac{y}{|y|}\right)
, where
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\backslash\{0\}
and
y_{n+1}\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore

\langle\widetilde{\nabla}h,(z,0)\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\nabla}h,(0,1)\rangle=0

on
\mathbb{S}^{n}\times\{1\}
, which implies
\widetilde{\nabla}h=(Dh,0)
on
\mathbb{S}^{n}\times\{1\}
. Hence,

(7.54)

\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}h((v,0),(w,0))=D^{2}h(v,w),\quad\forall v,w\in T_{z}\mathbb{S}^{n},

where the left-hand side was evaluated at
(z,1)
, and the right-hand side was evaluated at
z
. Using (
7.54
) and (
7.53
), we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}h=

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\langle\widetilde{\nabla}h,\nabla_{i}(z,1)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}^{2}h(\nabla_{i}(z,1),\nabla_{j}(z,1))+\langle\widetilde{\nabla}h,\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}(z,1)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D^{2}h(\nabla_{i}z,\nabla_{j}z)+\langle(Dh,0),\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}(z,1)\rangle

(7.55)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{j})+\langle(Dh,0),\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}(z,1)\rangle.

Formula (
2.8
) and
\langle(Dh,0),(z,1)\rangle=0
imply
\langle\left(Dh,0\right),X-\nu\rangle=0
. Inserting (
3.33
) into (
7.55
) therefore yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}h=

\displaystyle D^{2}h\left(e_{i},e_{j}\right)+\big\langle(Dh,0),-\varphi\nabla^{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}\partial_{l}X+\varphi\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{lj}\nu\big\rangle

(7.56)

\displaystyle-\varphi^{2}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{j}{}^{m}\langle V,\partial_{m}X\rangle\langle(Dh,0),\partial_{l}X\rangle-\varphi^{2}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{m}\tilde{h}_{j}{}^{l}\langle V,\partial_{m}X\rangle\langle(Dh,0),\partial_{l}X\rangle.

By using (
2.11
) and (
2.10
), we have

(7.57)

\displaystyle\langle(Dh,0),-\varphi\nabla^{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}\partial_{l}X\rangle=

\displaystyle\varphi\nabla^{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}A_{lm}[\varphi]D_{m}h,

(7.58)

\displaystyle\langle(Dh,0),\varphi\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{lj}\nu\rangle=

\displaystyle-\varphi\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{lj}\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle.

By using (
3.42
) and (
3.43
), we have

(7.59)

-\varphi^{2}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{j}{}^{m}\langle V,\partial_{m}X\rangle\langle(Dh,0),\partial_{l}X\rangle=\frac{1}{\varphi}D_{i}hD_{j}\varphi.

Inserting (
7.57
), (
7.58
) and (
7.59
) into (
7.56
), we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}h=

\displaystyle D^{2}h\left(e_{i},e_{j}\right)+\varphi\nabla^{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}A_{lm}[\varphi]D_{m}h-\varphi\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{lj}\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle

(7.60)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varphi}\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{j}\rangle+\frac{1}{\varphi}\langle Dh,e_{j}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle.

Then

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}hv^{i}v^{j}=

\displaystyle D^{2}h\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}v^{i}e_{i},\sum_{j=1}^{n}v^{j}e_{j}\right)+\varphi\nabla^{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}A_{lm}[\varphi]\left(D_{m}h\right)v^{i}v^{j}

(7.61)

\displaystyle-\varphi\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{lj}\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle v^{i}v^{j}+\frac{2}{\varphi}\langle Dh,\sum_{i=1}^{n}v^{i}e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,\sum_{j=1}^{n}v^{j}e_{j}\rangle.

By (
3.42
) and the assumption
\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}v^{i}=\varepsilon\tilde{H}v^{j}
at
X(x_{0},t_{0})
, we have

(7.62)

\sum_{i=1}^{n}v^{i}(e_{i},0)=-\varphi v^{i}\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}\partial_{j}X+\frac{v^{i}D_{i}\varphi}{\varphi}(z,1)=-\varepsilon\varphi\tilde{H}v^{j}\partial_{j}X+\frac{v^{i}D_{i}\varphi}{\varphi}(z,1).

Note that formula (
2.11
) implies
\langle\partial_{j}X,(z,1)\rangle=0
. Therefore, by using (
7.62
),
\langle(z,1),(z,1)\rangle=0
and the assumption
v^{i}v^{j}g_{ij}=1
, we have

(7.63)

\langle\sum_{i=1}^{n}v^{i}(e_{i},0),\sum_{j=1}^{n}v^{j}(e_{j},0)\rangle=\varepsilon^{2}\varphi^{2}\tilde{H}^{2}v^{i}v^{j}\langle\partial_{i}X,\partial_{j}X\rangle=\varepsilon^{2}\varphi^{2}\tilde{H}^{2}.

Letting
\bar{v}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}v^{i}e_{i}/(\varepsilon\varphi\tilde{H})
be a unit length vector on
T_{z_{0}}\mathbb{S}^{n}
, we have from (
7.61
) that

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}hv^{i}v^{j}=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\varphi^{2}\tilde{H}^{2}D^{2}h(\bar{v},\bar{v})+\varphi\nabla^{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}A_{lm}[\varphi]D_{m}h

(7.64)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\varphi\tilde{H}^{2}\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle+2\varepsilon^{2}\varphi\tilde{H}^{2}\langle Dh,\bar{v}\rangle\langle D\varphi,\bar{v}\rangle.

Using (
2.17
) and (
2.15
), we have

(7.65)

g^{ij}=\left(\sum_{l=1}^{n}A_{il}[\varphi]A_{lj}[\varphi]\right)^{-1}=\varphi^{2}\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}\delta_{ij}.

Then by (
7.60
), we know

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Delta h=\varepsilon g^{ij}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}h=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\varphi^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})+\varepsilon\varphi\nabla^{l}\tilde{H}A_{lm}[\varphi]D_{m}h

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle|\tilde{A}|^{2}+\varepsilon\varphi^{2}\frac{2}{\varphi}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\varphi^{2}|\tilde{A}|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})+\varepsilon\varphi\nabla^{l}\tilde{H}A_{lm}[\varphi]D_{m}h

(7.66)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle|\tilde{A}|^{2}+2\varepsilon\varphi|\tilde{A}|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle.

Since
S_{ij}\geq 0
on
M_{t_{0}}
, and
S_{ij}v^{j}=0
at
X(x_{0},t_{0})
, we have
\nabla_{l}S_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}=0
at the point, and hence
\nabla_{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}=\varepsilon\nabla_{l}\tilde{H}
at
X(x_{0},t_{0})
of
M_{t_{0}}
. Inserting (
7.64
) and (
7.66
) into (
7.50
), we have

\displaystyle\left(I\right):=

\displaystyle-\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}(\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}h-\varepsilon\Delta hg_{ij})v^{i}v^{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\left(\left(\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}h\right)v^{i}v^{j}-\varepsilon\Delta h\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\left(\varphi^{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})\right)|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(\bar{v},\bar{v})\tilde{H}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})

(7.67)

\displaystyle+2\varepsilon\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle\right)|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\langle Dh,\bar{v}\rangle\langle D\varphi,\bar{v}\rangle\tilde{H}^{2}\right).

Part 2
. In this part, we calculate (
7.51
). A straightforward calculation yields that

(7.68)

\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)\nabla_{j}h=-\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1}\nabla_{i}\varphi\nabla_{j}h=-\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1}D_{i}\varphi D_{j}h.

The following calculations are similar to those in (
7.64
) and (
7.66
). Using (
7.68
) and (
7.63
), we have

\displaystyle-2\Phi\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)\left(\nabla_{j}h\right)v^{i}v^{j}=

\displaystyle 2\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1}\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle\langle Dh,e_{j}\rangle v^{i}v^{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1}\langle D\varphi,\sum_{i=1}^{n}v^{i}e_{i}\rangle\langle Dh,\sum_{j=1}^{n}v^{j}e_{j}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1}\varepsilon^{2}\varphi^{2}\tilde{H}^{2}\langle D\varphi,\bar{v}\rangle\langle Dh,\bar{v}\rangle

(7.69)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{2}\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\langle D\varphi,\bar{v}\rangle\langle Dh,\bar{v}\rangle\tilde{H}^{2}.

Using the assumption
v^{i}v^{j}g_{ij}=1
and (
7.65
), we obtain

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon\Phi\langle\nabla\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right),\nabla h\rangle g_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon\Phi\left(-\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1}D_{i}\varphi D_{j}hg^{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1}D_{i}\varphi D_{j}h\left(\varphi^{2}\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}\delta_{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}D_{i}\varphi D_{i}h\right)

(7.70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\right)|\tilde{A}|^{2}.

Substituting (
7.69
) and (
7.70
) into (
7.51
), we have

\displaystyle\left(II\right):=

\displaystyle-2\Phi\left(\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)\nabla_{j}h-\varepsilon\langle\nabla\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right),\nabla h\rangle g_{ij}\right)v^{i}v^{j}

(7.71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle\right)|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\langle Dh,\bar{v}\rangle\langle D\varphi,\bar{v}\rangle\tilde{H}^{2}\right).

Part 3
. In this part, we calculate (
7.52
). For the term
\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)=\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)^{\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)+\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+2}\nabla_{i}\left(\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)\nabla_{j}\left(\frac{1}{\varphi}\right).

This together with (
3.24
), (
3.40
) and (
3.41
) yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}(-\nabla^{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}\langle V,\partial_{l}X\rangle-\frac{1}{\varphi}\tilde{h}_{ij}+\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\tilde{h}_{lj}\tilde{u})

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}-2}D_{i}\varphi D_{j}\varphi.

Hence, by using (
7.65
) and the assumption that
\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{j}v_{j}=\varepsilon\tilde{H}v_{i}
at
X(x_{0},t_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)v^{i}v^{j}=

\displaystyle-\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\left(\nabla^{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}\right)v^{i}v^{j}\langle V,\partial_{l}X\rangle-\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\tilde{H}

(7.72)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\tilde{u}\tilde{H}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\langle D\varphi,\bar{v}\rangle^{2}\tilde{H}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Delta\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\nabla^{l}\tilde{H}\langle V,\partial_{l}X\rangle

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\tilde{H}+\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\tilde{u}\tilde{|A|}^{2}

(7.73)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}\right)\tilde{|A|}^{2}.

Substituting (
7.72
) and (
7.73
) into (
7.52
) and using
\nabla^{l}\tilde{h}_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}=\varepsilon\nabla^{l}\tilde{H}
at
X(x_{0},t_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle\left(III\right):=

\displaystyle-\Phi h\left(\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)-\varepsilon\Delta\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\right)g_{ij}\right)v^{i}v^{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}h\tilde{u}(\tilde{|A|}^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})

(7.74)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}\right)\tilde{|A|}^{2}-\varepsilon\langle D\varphi,\bar{v}\rangle^{2}\tilde{H}^{2}\right).

Putting (
7.67
), (
7.71
) and (
7.74
) into (
7.49
) and using (
7.48
), we have

\displaystyle N_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\left(\varphi^{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})\right)|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(\bar{v},\bar{v})\tilde{H}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1)\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle\right)|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\langle Dh,\bar{v}\rangle\langle D\varphi,\bar{v}\rangle\tilde{H}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}h\tilde{u}(\tilde{|A|}^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}\right)\tilde{|A|}^{2}-\varepsilon\langle D\varphi,\bar{v}\rangle^{2}\tilde{H}^{2}\right)

(7.75)

\displaystyle+\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}(1-\varepsilon n)+\varepsilon\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})+\varepsilon\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}h(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2}).

If
M_{t}=\partial\Omega_{t}
is origin symmetric, then we recall the previous estimates of
||\varphi||_{C^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{n})}
and
\sup_{t\in[0,T)}\Phi(t)
in (
7.17
), (
7.22
) and (
7.23
). Since
\tilde{H}^{2}\leq n|\tilde{A}|^{2}
, the terms containing
\tilde{H}^{2}
(except
\varepsilon^{2}\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}\tilde{H}^{2}
) in the right-hand side of (
7.75
) can be bounded from below by
-\varepsilon^{2}C|\tilde{A}|^{2}
, where the constant
C
only depends on
n
,
k
,
p
,
\sup_{t\in[0,T)}\Phi(t)
,
||\varphi||_{C^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{n})}
and
||f||_{C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{n})}
; however, since (
3.16
) showed
\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}\geq 1
, the quantity
-\varepsilon^{2}C|\tilde{A}|^{2}
can be controlled by the positive term
\varepsilon\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})
when
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small. Hence, when
M_{t}=\partial\Omega_{t}
is origin symmetric, in order to prove
N_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}\geq 0
for small
\varepsilon
, we only need to focus on the coefficient of
|\tilde{A}|^{2}
in (
7.75
).

Case 1.

1\leq k\leq n-1
and
p>-n
. Recall the assumption that
M_{t}=\partial\Omega_{t}
is origin symmetric in this case. By the above argument, we only need to ensure the following inequality to be true, i.e.

\displaystyle\varphi^{2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})\right)-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle-2\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\varphi\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle\right)

(7.76)

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{n-k}\varphi h\tilde{u}+\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)h\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}\right)+h\geq 0.

Formula (
3.23
) yields

\varphi\tilde{u}=\frac{1}{2}|D\varphi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(\varphi^{2}-1).

Hence the inequality (
7.76
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle 0\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}h

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{i}^{2}}{|\tilde{A}|^{2}}\left(\varphi^{2}D^{2}h(e_{i},e_{i})-\varphi\langle Dh,D\varphi\rangle-\frac{2(k+p)}{n-k}\varphi\langle Dh,e_{i}\rangle\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}h|D\varphi|^{2}\right.

(7.77)

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{n+p}{2(n-k)}\varphi^{2}h+\frac{(n+p)(k+p)}{(n-k)^{2}}h\langle D\varphi,e_{i}\rangle^{2}+\frac{n-2k-p}{2(n-k)}h\right).

Since
\Omega_{t}
is origin symmetric, and
f(z)
satisfies Assumption
7.1
, we know that (
7.77
) follows from Lemma
7.6
.

Case 2.

0\leq k\leq n-1
and
p=-n
. In this case, we assumed that
f(z)
is a positive constant function on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
. Then (
7.75
) implies

N_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}=\dot{F}^{lm}\tilde{h}_{l}{}^{q}\tilde{h}_{qm}(1-\varepsilon n)+\varepsilon\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})+\varepsilon\Phi h(|\tilde{A}|^{2}-\varepsilon\tilde{H}^{2})\geq 0

for
\varepsilon
satisfying
0<\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{n}
.

Case 3.

k=0
and
-\infty<p<+\infty
. From
S_{ij}v^{i}=0
, we can easily get
\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(x_{0},t_{0})=\varepsilon\tilde{H}(x_{0},t_{0})
, where we assumed
\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\tilde{\kappa}_{n}
. Then we know
\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\leq\frac{(n-1)\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\tilde{\kappa}_{n}
, which implies

\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}\geq\frac{\partial p_{n}^{\frac{1}{n}}(\tilde{\kappa})}{\partial\tilde{\kappa}_{1}}=\frac{p_{n}^{\frac{1}{n}}(\tilde{\kappa})}{n\tilde{\kappa}_{1}}\geq\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{1}^{\frac{n-1}{n}}\tilde{\kappa}_{n}^{\frac{1}{n}}}{n\tilde{\kappa}_{1}}=\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{\tilde{\kappa}_{n}}{\tilde{\kappa}_{1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{n}}\geq\frac{1}{n(n-1)^{\frac{1}{n}}}\left(\frac{1-\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{n}}.

Hence we have that
\dot{F}^{lm}g_{lm}\to+\infty
as
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
. Recall that
M_{t}=\partial\Omega_{t}
is origin symmetric in Case 3.
By using (
7.75
) and the a priori estimates in Lemma
7.2
, Corollary
7.1
and Lemma
7.4
, we can choose a small
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(k,p,M_{0},||f||_{C^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{n})})>0
such that
N_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}\geq 0
.

In summary, with the assumptions of
n
,
k
,
p
,
f(z)
in Proposition
7.1
, there exists a small positive constant
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that
N_{ij}v^{i}v^{j}\geq 0
at
X(x_{0},t_{0})
. Hence we have
\tilde{h}_{ij}\geq\varepsilon_{0}\tilde{H}g_{ij}
along the flow (
7.7
), which implies

\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(x,t)\geq\varepsilon_{0}\tilde{H}(x,t)\geq\varepsilon_{0}(n-1)\tilde{\kappa}_{1}(x,t)+\varepsilon_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{n}(x,t),\quad\forall\ (x,t)\in M\times[0,T),

where we assumed
\tilde{\kappa}_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\tilde{\kappa}_{n}
at
X(x,t)
of
M_{t}
. This implies the desired inequality (
7.47
). We complete the proof of Proposition
7.1
.
∎

7.2.4.
Estimates of shifted principal curvatures

For abbreviation, let
\mathcal{F}(z,t)=p_{n-k}^{\frac{1}{n-k}}(A[\varphi(z,t)])
and
P(z,t)=\Phi(t)h(z)\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}(z,t)
. If
A[\varphi]>0
, then (
3.4
), (
3.7
) and (
3.11
) yield

(7.78)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(A[\varphi])=

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{F}}^{ij}A_{ij}[\varphi],

(7.79)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{F}^{ii}\geq

\displaystyle 1,

(7.80)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{ij}>

\displaystyle 0.

Recall that
\sigma_{ij}
denotes the metric on the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.
In the following lemmas, for a fixed pair
(z_{0},t_{0})\in\mathbb{S}^{n}\times[0,T)
, we use normal coordinates for
\mathbb{S}^{n}
around
z_{0}
such that
\sigma_{ij}(z_{0},t)=\delta_{ij}
for
t\in[0,T)
and
A_{ij}[\varphi(z_{0},t_{0})]
is diagonal.

Lemma 7.7
.

Along the scalar evolution equation (
7.11
) of
\varphi(z,t)
,
A_{ij}[\varphi(z,t)]
evolves as

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}A_{ij}[\varphi(z,t)]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{F}^{2}}D_{j}D_{i}\mathcal{F}-2\frac{\dot{\mathcal{F}}^{ij}}{\mathcal{F}^{3}}D_{i}\mathcal{F}D_{j}\mathcal{F}+\Phi D_{i}hD_{j}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)

\displaystyle+\Phi D_{j}hD_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)+\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}D_{j}D_{i}h-\Phi\langle D\varphi,Dh\rangle\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle-\frac{\langle D\varphi,D\mathcal{F}\rangle}{\varphi\mathcal{F}^{2}}\delta_{ij}-\frac{\cosh r}{\varphi\mathcal{F}}\delta_{ij}-\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)PA_{ij}[\varphi]

(7.81)

\displaystyle+\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\frac{P\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}{\varphi}+\frac{n+p}{n-k}P\tilde{u}\delta_{ij}+\frac{P}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}.

Proof.

From (
7.11
) we have

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\varphi(z,t)=\Phi(t)\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}(z,t)h(z)-\mathcal{F}^{-1}(z,t).

This together with (
2.12
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}A_{ij}[\varphi(z,t)]=

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\varphi(z,t))_{ij}-\frac{\langle D\varphi,D\partial_{t}\varphi(z,t)\rangle}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi^{2}}\partial_{t}\varphi(z,t)\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}\right)\partial_{t}\varphi(z,t)\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}h-\frac{1}{\mathcal{F}}\right)_{ij}-\frac{\langle D\varphi,D\left(\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}h-\frac{1}{\mathcal{F}}\right)\rangle}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi^{2}}\left(\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}h-\frac{1}{\mathcal{F}}\right)\delta_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{\varphi^{2}}\right)\left(\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}h-\frac{1}{\mathcal{F}}\right)\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi hD_{j}D_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)+\Phi D_{i}hD_{j}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)+\Phi D_{j}hD_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)

\displaystyle+\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}D_{j}D_{i}h+\frac{D_{j}D_{i}\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{F}^{2}}-2\frac{D_{i}\mathcal{F}D_{j}\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{F}^{3}}-\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\langle D\varphi,Dh\rangle\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle-\Phi h\frac{\langle D\varphi,D\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)\rangle}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}-\frac{\langle D\varphi,D\mathcal{F}\rangle}{\varphi\mathcal{F}^{2}}\delta_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\Phi h\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}

(7.82)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\varphi\mathcal{F}}\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi+\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)\Phi h\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\delta_{ij}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi+\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)\frac{1}{\varphi\mathcal{F}}\delta_{ij}.

A direct calculation shows

\displaystyle\Phi hD_{j}D_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)=

\displaystyle\Phi hD_{j}\left(\left(1-\frac{n+p}{n-k}\right)\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}D_{i}\varphi\right)

(7.83)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\Phi h\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\varphi_{ij}+\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\Phi h\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1}\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}

and

(7.84)

\displaystyle-\Phi h\frac{\langle D\varphi,D\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)\rangle}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}=\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\Phi h\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}.

Since
P(z,t)=\Phi(t)h(z)\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}(z,t)
, by using (
7.83
) and (
7.84
), we have

\displaystyle\Phi hD_{j}D_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)-\Phi h\frac{\langle D\varphi,D\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)\rangle}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\Phi h\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi+\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)\Phi h\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)P\varphi_{ij}+\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\frac{P\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}{\varphi}+\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-\frac{1}{2}\right)P\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}P\left(\varphi+\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)\delta_{ij}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)P\left(\varphi_{ij}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\varphi-\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)\delta_{ij}\right)+\frac{n+p}{n-k}\left(\frac{n+p}{n-k}-1\right)\frac{P\varphi_{i}\varphi_{j}}{\varphi}

(7.85)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\frac{n+p}{n-k}P\frac{|D\varphi|^{2}}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{n+p}{n-k}P\left(\varphi-\frac{1}{\varphi}\right)\delta_{ij}+\frac{P}{\varphi}\delta_{ij}.

Finally, plugging (
7.85
) into (
7.82
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}A_{ij}[\varphi]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathcal{F}^{2}}D_{j}D_{i}\mathcal{F}-\frac{2}{\mathcal{F}^{3}}D_{i}\mathcal{F}D_{j}\mathcal{F}+\Phi D_{i}hD_{j}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)

\displaystyle+\Phi D_{j}hD_{i}\left(\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}\right)+\Phi\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}+1}D_{j}D_{i}h-\Phi\langle D\varphi,Dh\rangle\varphi^{-\frac{n+p}{n-k}}\delta_{ij}
