are geodesic balls centered at the same point.

(2)

When
0\leq k\leq n-1
and
p=-n
, equality holds in (
10.26
) if and only if one of the following conditions holds,

(a)

K=L
,

(b)

K
and
L
are geodesic balls.

(3)

When
k=n
and
-n<p<0
, equality holds in (
10.26
) if and only if
K
and
L
are hyperbolic dilates.

(4)

When
k=n
and
p=-n
, equality always holds in (
10.26
).

By using (
2.16
), (
2.15
) and (
3.24
), we note that (
10.25
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\int_{\partial K}\left(\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)(\cosh r-\tilde{u})^{p}-1\right)p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\geq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-kr^{k}_{K}}\left(e^{p(r^{k}_{L}-r^{k}_{K})}-1\right),

and (
10.26
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\int_{\partial K}\left(\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)(\cosh r-\tilde{u})^{p}-1\right)p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\leq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-kr^{k}_{K}}\left(e^{p(r^{k}_{L}-r^{k}_{K})}-1\right).

Remark 10.2

(Relationship between Conjecture
10.5
and the flow (
7.7
))
.

We can assume
p>0
at first.
If we fix the smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain
K
in Conjecture
10.5
and set
f(z)=\varphi_{K}^{-p-n}(z)p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K}(G_{K}^{-1}(z)))
, then we have the left-hand side of (
10.25
) is non-increasing and the right-hand side of (
10.25
) is invariant along the flow (
7.7
) by (
7.14
) and (
7.13
), respectively. For the case
-n\leq p\leq 0
, the argument is roughly the same. Hence, flow (
7.7
) is a possible tool to solve Conjecture
10.5
.

In the case
\frac{1}{2}\leq p\leq 2
, we will show Conjecture
10.5
can be deduced from Conjecture
10.3
in the following way.

Proposition 10.3
.

Let
n
,
k
,
p
,
K
and
L
satisfy the assumptions in Conjecture
10.3
.
Assume that
\Omega_{t}:=(1-t)\cdot K+_{p}t\cdot L
. Then Conjecture
10.5
is equivalent to

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\exp(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})))\geq\exp(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(L)))-\exp(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(K))).

Thus Conjecture
10.5
can be obtained from Conjecture
10.3
in this case.

Proof.

Denote by
u(z,t)
the horospherical support function of
\Omega_{t}
, and
\varphi(z,t)=e^{u(z,t)}
. Then we have from the definition of
\Omega_{t}
that

\varphi^{p}(z,t)=(1-t)\varphi^{p}_{K}(z)+t\varphi^{p}_{L}(z).

Hence

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\right|_{t=0}u(z,t)=\frac{1}{p}\frac{\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)-\varphi_{K}^{p}(z)}{\varphi_{K}^{p}(z)}.

Applying a similar calculation as in (
10.22
), we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\exp\left(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t}))\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle pe^{pr^{k}_{K}}\left(\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-kr^{k}_{K}}\right)^{-1}\int_{\partial K}\left(\frac{1}{p}\frac{\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)-\varphi_{K}^{p}(z)}{\varphi_{K}^{p}(z)}\right)p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-(k+p)r^{k}_{K}}\right)^{-1}\left(\int_{\partial K}\varphi_{L}^{p}\varphi_{K}^{-p}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu-\int_{\partial K}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-(k+p)r^{k}_{K}}\right)^{-1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}\varphi_{K}^{-(n+p)}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})d\sigma\right),

where we used (
2.16
) and (
2.15
) in the last equality. Hence, by use of (
10.3
), we can rewrite Conjecture
10.5
as

(10.27)

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\exp(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})))\geq e^{pr^{k}_{L}}-e^{pr^{k}_{K}}=\exp(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(L)))-\exp(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(K))).

On the other hand, taking
a=1-t
and
b=t
in Conjecture
10.3
yields

(10.28)

\exp(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})))\geq(1-t)\exp(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(K)))+t\exp(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(L))).

Hence (
10.27
) can be obtained by taking the derivative of (
10.28
) with respect to
t
at
t=0
.
∎

However, if we set
a=1
,
b=t\geq 0
in Conjecture
10.3
and take the derivative of (
10.7
) with respect to
t
at
t=0
, then we can get the following conjecture by a similar method as in the proof of Proposition
10.3
. Here we omit calculations in details.

Conjecture 10.6

(Horospherical
p
-Minkowski inequalities of type II)
.

Let
n\geq 1
and
0\leq k\leq n
be integers, and let
p
be a positive number.
Suppose that
K
and
L
are two smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domains in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
. Then

(10.29)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}\varphi_{K}^{-p-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})d\sigma\geq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-(k+p)r^{k}_{K}}e^{pr^{k}_{L}},

where
r^{k}_{\Omega}
is defined by
r^{k}_{\Omega}=I_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega))
for a h-convex bounded domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
.

(1)

When
0\leq k\leq n-1
, equality holds in (
10.29
) if and only if
K
and
L
are geodesic balls centered at the same point.

(2)

When
k=n
, equality holds in (
10.29
) if and only if
K=c\cdot L
or
L=c\cdot K
for some constant
c\geq 1
.

By using (
2.16
), (
2.15
) and (
3.24
), we note that (
10.29
) is equivalent to

\int_{\partial K}\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)(\cosh r-\tilde{u})^{p}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\geq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-(k+p)r^{k}_{K}}e^{pr^{k}_{L}}.

Remark 10.3
.

It is easy to see that (
10.29
) follows from (
10.25
) and (
10.18
). Hence Conjecture
10.6
is weaker than Conjecture
10.5
.

When
L=B(r_{L})
, by (
10.3
), we have
\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)=e^{pr_{L}}=e^{pr^{k}_{L}}
for all
z\in\mathbb{S}^{n}
. Hence, if
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin, then Conjecture
10.6
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\int_{\partial K}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})^{p}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\geq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-(k+p)r^{k}_{K}},

where
n\geq 1
,
0\leq k\leq n
,
p>0
and
K
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
.

10.2.
Horospherical
p
-Minkowski inequalities

In this subsection, we discuss Conjecture
10.5
and Conjecture
10.6
. For fixed
n
,
k
and
p
, we first show that these conjectures only depend on the relative position of smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domains
K
and
L
.

Proposition 10.4
.

Let
n\geq 1
and
0\leq k\leq n
be integers, and let
p
be a real number.
Assume that
K
and
L
are smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domains in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
.
Then the integral

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)\varphi_{K}^{-p-n}(z)p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})d\sigma

only depends on the relative position of
K
and
L
. Namely, we have for any isometry
f
of
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{f(L)}^{p}(z)\varphi_{f(K)}^{-p-n}(z)p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{f(K)})d\sigma=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)\varphi_{K}^{-p-n}(z)p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})d\sigma.

Proof.

In this proof, we let
f
be an isometry of
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
. It is well-known that
f\in O^{+}(n+1,1)
, i.e.
f
is a linear isomorphism of
\mathbb{R}^{n+1,1}
that preserves the Lorentzian metric and
\{Y=(y,y_{n+1})\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1,1}:y_{n+1}>0\}
, see e.g.
[
63
, p. 47]
.
For any fixed
X\in\partial K
, we let
z=G_{K}(X)
, where
G_{K}
is the horospherical Gauss map of
K
. Then we have that the supporting horosphere of
K
at
X
is given by

\displaystyle H_{z}(u_{K}(z))=\{Y=(y,y_{n+1})\in\mathbb{H}^{n+1}:\langle-Y,(z,1)\rangle=\varphi_{K}(z)\}.

Since
f
preserves the Lorentzian metric, condition
\langle-Y,(z,1)\rangle=\varphi_{K}(z)
is equivalent to
\langle-f(Y),f\left((z,1)\right)\rangle=\varphi_{K}(z)
. Besides, it is easy to see that
f\left((z,1)\right)
lies in the future light cone of
(0,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1,1}
.
Then we have

(10.30)

\displaystyle f\left(H_{z}(u_{K}(z))\right)=\{Y\in\mathbb{H}^{n+1}:\langle-Y,(\tilde{z},1)\rangle=\chi(z)\varphi_{K}(z)\},

where
\chi(z)
and
\tilde{z}
were uniquely determined by
\chi(z)f\left((z,1)\right)=\left(\tilde{z},1\right)
.
Let
\widetilde{K}=f(K)
and
\widetilde{X}=f(X)
. We know that
f\left(H_{z}(u_{K}(z))\right)
is the supporting horosphere of
\widetilde{K}
at
\widetilde{X}
and
G_{\widetilde{K}}(\widetilde{X})=\tilde{z}
. By using (
10.30
), we obtain

\varphi_{\widetilde{K}}(\tilde{z})=\chi(z)\varphi_{K}(z)
.
Since
\chi(z)
only depends on the choice of
f
, we have
\varphi_{\widetilde{L}}(\tilde{z})=\chi(z)\varphi_{L}(z)
by the same argument. That gives

(10.31)

\displaystyle\frac{\varphi_{\widetilde{L}}(\tilde{z})}{\varphi_{\widetilde{K}}(\tilde{z})}=\frac{\varphi_{L}(z)}{\varphi_{K}(z)}.

Since
f
is an isometry of
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
, we have

(10.32)

\displaystyle p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa}_{K})d\mu_{\partial K}=f^{*}\left(p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa}_{\widetilde{K}})d\mu_{\partial\widetilde{K}}\right).

Combining (
10.31
) with (
10.32
) yields

(10.33)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial K}\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)\varphi_{K}^{-p}(z)p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{\partial K}=\int_{\partial\widetilde{K}}\varphi_{\widetilde{L}}^{p}(\tilde{z})\varphi_{\widetilde{K}}^{-p}(\tilde{z})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{\partial\widetilde{K}}.

Then Proposition
10.4
follows by using (
2.16
) and (
2.15
) in (
10.33
).
∎

Corollary 10.2
.

Assume that
n
,
k
,
p
satisfy the assumptions in Conjecture
10.5
(or Conjecture
10.6
). Then Conjecture
10.5
(or Conjecture
10.6
) only depends on the relative position of the smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domains
K
and
L
. Equivalently, the following statements are equivalent,

(1)

Conjecture
10.5
(or Conjecture
10.6
) holds for
K
and
L
,

(2)

Conjecture
10.5
(or Conjecture
10.6
) holds for
f(K)
and
f(L)
,

where
f
is any isometry of
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
.

Proof.

Let
f\in O^{+}(n+1,1)
be any isometry of
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
.
By using (
2.16
) and (
2.15
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})d\sigma=\int_{\partial K}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{K},

which is preserved along
f
.
Besides, we know that the modified quermassintegrals of h-convex bounded domains are preserved along
f
, i.e.
\widetilde{W}_{k}(K)=\widetilde{W}_{k}(f(K))
and
\widetilde{W}_{k}(L)=\widetilde{W}_{k}(f(L))
for all
k=0,1,\ldots,n
. Then Corollary
10.2
follows directly from Proposition
10.4
.
∎

10.2.1.
Results about Conjecture
10.5

We will consider Conjecture
10.5
in several cases at first. When
K=L
, Conjecture
10.5
is trivial.

(1)

When
n\geq 1
and
k=n
, we will show that Conjecture
10.5
is equivalent to the Hölder inequalities in Theorem
10.7
.

(2)

When
K
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin and
L
is a general smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain, Conjecture
10.5
holds, which will be proved in Theorem
10.8
.

(3)

When
L
is a geodesic ball and
K
is general, we prove Conjecture
10.5
in Theorem
10.9
with assumption
p=1
.

Theorem 10.7
.

Conjecture
10.5
holds in the case
n\geq 1
and
k=n
.

Proof.

Let
u_{K}(z)
and
u_{L}(z)
be the horospherical support functions of
K
and
L
respectively, and let
\varphi_{K}(z)=e^{u_{K}(z)}
and
\varphi_{L}(z)=e^{u_{L}(z)}
.

Case 1.

p>0
. By the Hölder inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{-n}d\sigma\right)^{\frac{p}{n+p}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}\varphi_{K}^{-n-p}d\sigma\right)^{\frac{n}{n+p}},

with equality if and only if
\varphi_{L}^{-1}(z)\varphi_{K}(z)
is constant on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.
We rewrite the above inequality as

(10.34)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}\varphi_{K}^{-n-p}d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\geq\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\right)\left(\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{-n}d\sigma\right)^{-\frac{p}{n}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\right)^{\frac{p}{n}}-1\right).

By (
10.10
), we have

(10.35)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma=\omega_{n}e^{-nr^{n}_{K}},\quad\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{-n}d\sigma=\omega_{n}e^{-nr^{n}_{L}}.

Substituting (
10.35
) into (
10.34
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}\varphi_{K}^{-n-p}d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\geq\omega_{n}e^{-nr^{n}_{K}}\left(e^{p(r^{n}_{L}-r^{n}_{K})}-1\right),

thus we obtain (
10.25
) in the case
n\geq 1
,
k=0
and
p>0
.
Equality holds if and only if
K
and
L
are hyperbolic dilates.

Case 2.

-n<p<0
. By the Hölder inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}\varphi_{K}^{-n-p}d\sigma\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{-n}d\sigma\right)^{-\frac{p}{n}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\right)^{\frac{p}{n}+1},

with equality if and only if
\varphi_{L}^{-1}(z)\varphi_{K}(z)
is constant on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
.
We rewrite the above inequality as

(10.36)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}\varphi_{K}^{-n-p}d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\right)\left(\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{-n}d\sigma\right)^{-\frac{p}{n}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\right)^{\frac{p}{n}}-1\right).

Substituting (
10.35
) into (
10.36
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}\varphi_{K}^{-n-p}d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma\leq\omega_{n}e^{-nr^{n}_{K}}\left(e^{p(r^{n}_{L}-r^{n}_{K})}-1\right),

thus we obtain (
10.26
) in the case
n\geq 1
,
k=n
and
-n<p<0
.
Equality holds if and only if
K
and
L
are hyperbolic dilates.

Case 3.

p=-n
. Using (
10.35
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{-n}d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}d\sigma=\omega_{n}e^{-nr^{n}_{K}}\left(e^{-n\left(r^{n}_{L}-r^{n}_{K}\right)}-1\right),

thus we obtain that equality always holds in (
10.26
) when
n\geq 1
,
k=n
and
p=-n
.
We complete the proof of Theorem
10.7
.
∎

If
n\geq 1
,
k=n
and
p>0
, then by use of (
10.35
),
it is easy to see that Conjecture
10.6
is equivalent to Conjecture
10.5
in this case. Then we get the following Corollary
10.3
.

Corollary 10.3
.

Conjecture
10.6
holds in the case
n\geq 1
and
k=n
.

If we let
K=B(r)
be a geodesic ball of radius
r>0
centered at the origin, then it is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\varphi_{K}^{-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})=

\displaystyle\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-kr^{k}_{K}},

\displaystyle\varphi_{K}^{-p-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})=

\displaystyle\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-(k+p)r^{k}_{K}}.

Therefore inequalities (
10.25
) and (
10.26
) in Conjecture
10.5
are equivalent to

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}d\sigma\geq

\displaystyle\omega_{n}e^{pr^{k}_{L}},\quad p>0,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}d\sigma\leq

\displaystyle\omega_{n}e^{pr^{k}_{L}},\quad-n\leq p<0

respectively, where
n\geq 1
,
0\leq k\leq n
, and
L
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
. In the next theorem, we will prove Conjecture
10.5
in the case that
K=B(r)
.

Theorem 10.8
.

Conjecture
10.5
holds when
K
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin. Precisely, if we assume
n\geq 1
,
0\leq k\leq n
,
p\geq-n
and
L
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
, then

(10.37)

\left(\frac{\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}d\sigma}{\omega_{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\geq e^{r^{k}_{L}}.

(1)

When
0\leq k\leq n-1
and
p>-n
, equality holds if and only if
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

(2)

When
0\leq k\leq n-1
and
p=-n
, equality holds if and only if
L
is a geodesic ball.

(3)

When
k=n
and
p>-n
, equality holds if and only if
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

(4)

When
k=n
and
p=-n
, equality always holds.

Proof.

Using the Hölder inequality (see e.g.,
[
38
, p. 146]
), (
10.10
) and Theorem C, we have

(10.38)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{p}d\sigma}{\omega_{n}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\geq\left(\frac{\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{L}^{-n}d\sigma}{\omega_{n}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{n}}=e^{r^{n}_{L}}\geq e^{r^{k}_{L}},

where
p\geq-n
. Thus we obtain (
10.37
). Now we characterize the equality cases of (
10.37
).

If
0\leq k\leq n
and
p>-n
, then equality holds in the first inequality of (
10.38
) if and only if
\varphi_{L}(z)
is constant on
\mathbb{S}^{n}
, which means that
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin. By Theorem C, equality holds in the second inequality of (
10.38
) when
L
is a geodesic ball. Hence the Case (1) and the Case (3) in Theorem
10.8
are proved.

If
0\leq k\leq n-1
and
p=-n
, then Theorem C implies that equality holds in the second inequality of (
10.38
) if and only if
L
is a geodesic ball. Thus Case (2) is proved.
Case (4) follows directly form (
10.10
).
We complete the proof of Theorem
10.8
.
∎

Remark 10.4
.

Taking
p\to 0^{+}
in (
10.37
), the corresponding inequality is

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}u_{L}(z)d\sigma\geq\omega_{n}r^{k}_{L},

equality holds if and only if
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin,
where
u_{L}(z)
is the horospherical support function of a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain
L
.

Since equality holds in (
10.18
) when
\Omega
is a geodesic ball, the following Corollary
10.4
is a direct consequence of Theorem
10.8
, see Remark
10.3
.

Corollary 10.4
.

Conjecture
10.6
holds when
K
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

In
[
45
]
, Hu et al. introduced the following locally constrained curvature flow,

(10.39)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{\partial}{\partial t}X(x,t)=&\left(\left(\cosh r-\tilde{u}\right)\frac{p_{k-1}(\tilde{\kappa})}{p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})}-\tilde{u}\right)\nu,\quad k=1,\ldots,n,\\
X(\cdot,0)=&M_{0},\end{aligned}\right.

They proved the long time existence and convergence for flow (
10.39
) in the following Theorem D. Here we only focus on the case that
M_{0}
is uniformly h-convex.

Theorem D

(
[
45
]
)
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
1\leq k\leq n
, and let
M_{0}=\partial\Omega_{0}
be a smooth uniformly h-convex hypersurface in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
such that
\Omega_{0}
contains the origin in its interior. Then the flow (
10.39
) has a smooth solution for all time
t\in[0,+\infty)
, and
M_{t}
is uniformly h-convex for each
t>0
and converges smoothly and exponentially to a geodesic sphere of radius
r_{\infty}
such that
\widetilde{W}_{k}(B_{r_{\infty}})=\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{0})
.

Using the flow (
10.39
), Hu et al.
[
45
]
gave an alternative proof of Theorem C. Furthermore, they proved the following weighted Alexandrov-Fenchel inequalities. We state their result by use of the modified
k
-mean radius defined in (
10.3
).

Theorem E

(
[
45
]
)
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
1\leq k\leq n
be integers. Assume that
\Omega
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
which contains the origin in its interior. Then there holds

(10.40)

\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\geq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{\Omega}e^{-(k+1)r^{k}_{\Omega}}.

Equality holds if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

Hu et al.
[
45
]
found that the left-hand side of (
10.40
) is monotone non-increasing along the flow (
10.39
), while the right-hand side of (
10.40
) is invariant along the flow. Hence they proved Theorem E by applying the convergence result in Theorem D. In the following Lemma
10.3
, we find a new geometric quantity which is also monotone non-increasing along the flow (
10.39
).

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
1\leq k\leq n-1
be integers. Along the flow (
10.39
), we have

(10.41)

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\left(\frac{c_{0}}{\varphi(z,t)}-1\right)\varphi^{-n}(z,t)p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}(G_{t}^{-1}(z)))d\sigma\leq 0,

for any fixed number
c_{0}\geq 1
, which is equivalent to

(10.42)

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{M_{t}}\left({c_{0}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})-1\right)p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}\leq 0.

Equality holds if and only if
M_{t}
is a geodesic sphere centered at the origin.

Proof.

The equivalence of (
10.41
) and (
10.42
) follows from (
2.16
), (
2.15
) and (
3.24
).
The following evolution equation was calculated in
[
45
, Section 9.1]
,

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{M_{t}}c_{0}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}=c_{0}\int_{M_{t}}\left((n-k)\cosh rp_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})-(k+1)(\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\right)\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t},

where
\mathscr{F}
represents the speed function of the flow (
10.39
). Taking the time derivative of (
5.2
) and using Lemma
5.1
, we have

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{{M_{t}}}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}=(n-2k)\int_{M_{t}}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}+(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{M_{t}}\left({c_{0}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})-1\right)p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{0}(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}\cosh rp_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}-(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}

(10.43)

\displaystyle-c_{0}(k+1)\int_{M_{t}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}-(n-2k)\int_{{M_{t}}}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}.

Since in the flow (
10.39
),

(10.44)

\mathscr{F}=(\cosh r-\tilde{u})\frac{p_{k-1}(\tilde{\kappa})}{p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})}-\tilde{u},

we have

\displaystyle c_{0}(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}\cosh rp_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}-(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{0}(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}\cosh r\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})\frac{p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})p_{k-1}(\tilde{\kappa})}{p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})}-p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t}

(10.45)

\displaystyle-(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})\frac{p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})p_{k-1}(\tilde{\kappa})}{p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})}-p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t}.

As
c_{0}\geq 1
, we have
c_{0}(n-k)\cosh r-(n-k)\geq(c_{0}-1)(n-k)\geq 0
. Using (
3.5
) in the right-hand side of (
10.45
), we get

\displaystyle c_{0}(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}\cosh rp_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}-(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{0}(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}\cosh r\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})-p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle-(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})-p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t}

(10.46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{0}(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}\cosh r\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})-p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t},

where we used (
3.20
) in the equality.
Using (
10.44
) in the last line of (
10.43
), we have

\displaystyle-c_{0}(k+1)\int_{M_{t}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}-(n-2k)\int_{{M_{t}}}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\mathscr{F}d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-c_{0}(k+1)\int_{M_{t}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k-1}(\tilde{\kappa})-p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle-(n-2k)\int_{M_{t}}\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k-1}(\tilde{\kappa})-p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t}

(10.47)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-c_{0}(k+1)\int_{M_{t}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k-1}(\tilde{\kappa})-p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t},

where we used (
3.20
) in the last equality.
Substituting (
10.46
) and (
10.47
) into (
10.43
) yields

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{M_{t}}\left({c_{0}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})-1\right)p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{0}(n-k)\int_{M_{t}}\cosh r\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})-p_{k+1}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle-c_{0}(k+1)\int_{M_{t}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})\left((\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k-1}(\tilde{\kappa})-p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{u}\right)d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{0}\frac{n-k}{k+1}\int_{M_{t}}\cosh r\dot{p}_{k+1}^{ij}(\tilde{\kappa})\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\cosh rd\mu_{t}-c_{0}\frac{k+1}{k}\int_{M_{t}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})\dot{p}_{k}^{ij}(\tilde{\kappa})\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\cosh rd\mu_{t},

where we used (
3.21
) in the equality. Since
\dot{p}_{k+1}^{ij}(\tilde{\kappa})
and
\dot{p}_{k}^{ij}(\tilde{\kappa})
are positive definite and divergence-free, integration by parts gives

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{M_{t}}\left({c_{0}}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})-1\right)p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-c_{0}\frac{n-k}{k+1}\int_{M_{t}}\dot{p}_{k+1}^{ij}(\tilde{\kappa})\nabla_{i}\cosh r\nabla_{j}\cosh rd\mu_{t}+c_{0}\frac{k+1}{k}\int_{M_{t}}\dot{p}_{k}^{ij}(\tilde{\kappa})\nabla_{i}\cosh r\nabla_{j}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-c_{0}\frac{n-k}{k+1}\int_{M_{t}}\dot{p}_{k+1}^{ij}(\tilde{\kappa})\langle V,\partial_{i}X\rangle\langle V,\partial_{j}X\rangle d\mu_{t}-c_{0}\frac{k+1}{k}\int_{M_{t}}\dot{p}_{k}^{ij}(\tilde{\kappa})\tilde{h}_{i}{}^{l}\langle V,\partial_{l}X\rangle\langle V,\partial_{j}X\rangle d\mu_{t}

(10.48)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0,

where we used (
3.40
) in the equality. Then obtain (
10.42
).

By the uniform h-convexity of
M_{t}
and (
3.39
), equality holds in the last inequality of (
10.48
) if and only if
\nabla\cosh r=0
, which means that
M_{t}
is a geodesic sphere centered at the origin. This completes the proof of Lemma
10.3
.
∎

Using the new monotone quantity of the flow (
10.39
) proved in Lemma
10.3
, we can prove that Conjecture
10.5
holds in a special case in the following Theorem
10.9
.

Theorem 10.9
.

Conjecture
10.5
holds when
n\geq 2
,
1\leq k\leq n-1
,
p=1
,
K
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain which contains the origin in its interior, and
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin. Precisely, if
L=B(r_{L})
, then

(10.49)

\displaystyle e^{r_{L}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-1-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})d\sigma-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}\varphi_{K}^{-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})d\sigma\geq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-kr^{k}_{K}}\left(e^{(r_{L}-r^{k}_{K})}-1\right)

for any smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain
K
which contains the origin in its interior. Particularly, when
L
degenerates to the origin, we have

(10.50)

\displaystyle\int_{\partial K}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu-\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-(k+1)r^{k}_{K}}\geq\int_{\partial K}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu-\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-kr^{k}_{K}}\geq 0.

In the both inequalities, equality holds if and only if
K
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

Proof.

Taking
\Omega_{0}=K
in Theorem D, as the flow (
10.39
) preserves
\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})
, we have
r_{\infty}=r^{k}_{K}
. Taking
c_{0}=e^{r_{L}}\geq 1
in Lemma
10.3
and using Theorem D, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n}}(e^{r_{L}}\varphi_{K}^{-1}-1)\varphi_{K}^{-n}p_{n-k}(\tilde{\lambda}_{K})d\sigma\geq

\displaystyle\omega_{n}(e^{r_{L}}e^{-r_{\infty}}-1)e^{-nr_{\infty}}\sinh^{n-k}r_{\infty}e^{(n-k)r_{\infty}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{n}(e^{r_{L}}e^{-r^{k}_{K}}-1)e^{-nr^{k}_{K}}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{(n-k)r^{k}_{K}},

and hence obtain (
10.49
). Equality holds in (
10.49
) if and only if
K
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

By using (
2.16
), (
2.15
) and (
3.24
), we have that inequality (
10.49
) is equivalent to

e^{r_{L}}\int_{\partial K}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu-\int_{\partial K}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\geq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}(e^{r_{L}-r^{k}_{K}}-1)e^{-kr^{k}_{K}}.

Taking
r_{L}=0
in the above inequality and using (
10.18
), we obtain (
10.50
). We complete the proof of Theorem
10.9
.
∎

Remark 10.5
.

We notice that Theorem
10.9
implies Theorem E directly.

10.2.2.
Results about Conjecture
10.6

In the last part of this subsection, we will study Conjecture
10.6
. Although Conjecture
10.6
can be obtained form Conjecture
10.5
by using (
10.18
), Conjecture
10.5
has not been proved in general case. It makes sense for us to discuss Conjecture
10.6
here.

Let
K
contain the origin and
L
be a geodesic ball centered at the origin in Conjecture
10.6
. By Theorem
10.9
or Theorem E, we have Conjecture
10.6
holds for
n\geq 2
,
1\leq k\leq n-1
,
p=1
in this case.
Now we prove Conjecture
10.6
in the case
n\geq 2
,
1\leq k\leq n-1
and
p\geq 1
by using Theorem
10.9
.

Proposition 10.5
.

Let
n\geq 2
,
1\leq k\leq n-1
be integers,
p\geq 1
be a positive number and
\Omega\subset\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
be a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain which contains the origin in its interior, then

\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})^{p}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\geq\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)e^{-pr^{k}_{\Omega}}.

Equality holds if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

Proof.

By Theorem
10.9
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu-\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{\Omega}e^{-kr^{k}_{\Omega}}(1-e^{-r^{k}_{K}})

(10.51)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\partial\Omega}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\right)e^{-r^{k}_{\Omega}},

where the second inequality follows from (
10.18
). Equality holds if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.
Hence the
p=1
case of Proposition
10.5
is proved. From the Hölder inequality and (
10.51
), we can easily get

\left(\frac{\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})^{p}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu}{\int_{\partial\Omega}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\geq\frac{\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu}{\int_{\partial\Omega}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu}\geq e^{-r^{k}_{\Omega}}

for
p\geq 1
. Equality holds if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.
This completes the proof of Proposition
10.5
.
∎

Using (
10.18
) in Proposition
10.5
, we can get the following Theorem
10.10
.

Theorem 10.10
.

Conjecture
10.6
holds in the case
n\geq 2
,
1\leq k\leq n-1
,
p\geq 1
,
K
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain which contains the origin in its interior and
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin. Precisely, we have

(10.52)

\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})^{p}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\geq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{\Omega}e^{-(k+p)r^{k}_{\Omega}},

where
\Omega
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain which contains the origin in its interior.
Equality holds in (
10.52
) if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

Now we study the case
n\geq 1
,
k=0
and
p\geq 1
in Conjecture
10.6
. The following Proposition
10.6
will give a comparison between weighted volume and the canonical volume of bounded domains in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
.

Proposition 10.6
.

Let
\Omega
be a bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}

(n\geq 1)
, then we have

(10.53)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)=\int_{\Omega}\cosh rdv\geq\frac{\omega_{n}}{n+1}\sinh^{n+1}r^{0}_{\Omega},

with equality if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

Proof.

It is easy to see that
\mathrm{Vol}\left(\Omega\backslash B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\right)=\mathrm{Vol}\left(B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\backslash\Omega\right)
by the definition of
r^{0}_{\Omega}
in (
10.3
).
Noting that
\cosh r\geq\cosh r^{0}_{\Omega}
on
\Omega\backslash B(r^{0}_{\Omega})
and
\cosh r\leq\cosh r^{0}_{\Omega}
in
B(r^{0}_{\Omega})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}\left(\Omega\cap B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\right)+\mathrm{Vol}_{w}\left(\Omega\backslash B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}\left(\Omega\cap B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\right)+\cosh r^{0}_{\Omega}\mathrm{Vol}\left(\Omega\backslash B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}\left(B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\cap\Omega\right)+\cosh r^{0}_{\Omega}\mathrm{Vol}\left(B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\backslash\Omega\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}_{w}\left(B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\cap\Omega\right)+\mathrm{Vol}_{w}\left(B(r^{0}_{\Omega}\backslash\Omega)\right)=\mathrm{Vol}_{w}\left(B(r^{0}_{\Omega})\right).

Equality holds if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.
∎

Here we state a weighted isoperimetric inequality proved by Scheuer and Xia
[
72
]
.

Theorem F

(
[
72
]
)
.

Let
\Omega
be a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}

(n\geq 1)
which contains the origin in its interior. Then

\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh rd\mu\geq\left(\left((n+1)\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)\right)^{2}+\omega_{n}^{\frac{2}{n+1}}\left((n+1)\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)\right)^{\frac{2n}{n+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Equality holds if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

Remark 10.6
.

We remark that Theorem F has been recently improved by the authors
[
54
]
that the above weighted isoperimetric inequality actually holds for any domain
\Omega
with
C^{1}
boundary. Besides, Theorem F was recently generalized to a class of weighted Alexandrov-Fenchel inequalities by Hu and the first author
[
47
]
.

In the following Theorem
10.11
, by using Theorem F and Proposition
10.6
, we will prove that (
10.40
) holds in the case
k=0
.

Theorem 10.11
.

Conjecture
10.6
holds in the case
n\geq 1
,
k=0
,
p=1
,
K
is a smooth h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
which contains the origin in its interior and
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.
Precisely, we have

(10.54)

\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})d\mu\geq\omega_{n}\sinh^{n}r^{0}_{\Omega}e^{-r^{0}_{\Omega}},

where
\Omega\subset\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain which contains the origin in its interior.
Equality holds in (
10.54
) if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

Proof.

By (
6.8
) and Theorem F, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})d\mu=\int_{\partial\Omega}\cosh rd\mu-(n+1)\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)

(10.55)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(\left((n+1)\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)\right)^{2}+\omega_{n}^{\frac{2}{n+1}}\left((n+1)\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)\right)^{\frac{2n}{n+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-(n+1)\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega).

It is easy to see that the right-hand side of (
10.55
) is monotone increasing as a single-variable function of
\mathrm{Vol}_{w}(\Omega)
. Then by using (
10.53
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\partial\Omega}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})d\mu\geq

\displaystyle\left(\left(\omega_{n}\sinh^{n+1}r^{0}_{\Omega}\right)^{2}+\omega_{n}^{\frac{2}{n+1}}\left(\omega_{n}\sinh^{n+1}r^{0}_{\Omega}\right)^{\frac{2n}{n+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\omega_{n}\sinh^{n+1}r^{0}_{\Omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{n}\sinh^{n}r^{0}_{\Omega}\left(\left(\sinh^{2}r^{0}_{\Omega}+1\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\sinh r^{0}_{\Omega}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{n}\sinh^{n}r^{0}_{\Omega}e^{-r^{0}_{\Omega}}.

Then we obtain (
10.54
) and the equality holds if and only if
\Omega
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin. Hence, Theorem
10.11
follows from (
10.54
) and Remark
10.3
.
∎

10.3.
Horospherical
p
-Brunn-Minkowski inequalities

In this subsection, we study the horospherical
p
-Brunn-Minkowski inequalities which we proposed in Conjecture
10.3
.

We have shown in Proposition
10.2
that Conjecture
10.3
can be obtained from the special case where
a\geq 0
,
b\geq 0
and
a+b=1
. We proved that Conjecture
10.3
holds for
k=n
in Theorem
10.4
. When
K=L
, we proved Conjecture
10.3
in Proposition
10.1
. In the following propositions, we will prove Conjecture
10.3
holds for

(1)

n\geq 2
,
1\leq k\leq n-1
,
1\leq p\leq 2
,
a=1
,
b\geq 0
,
K
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
which contains the origin in its interior and
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin,

(2)

n\geq 1
,
k=0
,
p=1
,
a=1
,
b\geq 0
,
K
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
which contains the origin in its interior and
L
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

Proposition 10.7
.

Conjecture
10.3
holds in the case
n\geq 2
,
1\leq k\leq n-1
,
1\leq p\leq 2
,
a=1
,
b=t\geq 0
,
K
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
which contains the origin in its interior and
L
is a geodesic ball of radius
r_{L}\geq 0
centered at the origin. Precisely, we have

(10.56)

\displaystyle\exp\left(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t}))\right)\geq\exp\left(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(K))\right)+t\exp\left(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(B(r_{L})))\right),

where
t\geq 0
and
\Omega_{t}=K+_{p}t\cdot B(r_{L})
. If
t>0
, then equality holds in (
10.56
) if and only if
K
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

Proof.

From Definition
1.1
, we have
\varphi_{\Omega_{t}}(z):=\varphi(z,t)=\left(\varphi_{K}^{p}(z)+t\varphi_{L}^{p}(z)\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}
. Hence, at
t=0
we have

\frac{\partial}{\partial t}u(z,t)=\varphi_{K}^{-1}\partial_{t}\varphi(z,t)=\frac{1}{p}\left(\frac{\varphi_{L}(z)}{\varphi_{K}(z)}\right)^{p}.

Therefore, by using Lemma
5.1
, (
3.24
) and
\varphi_{L}(z)\equiv e^{r_{L}}
, we have

(10.57)

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})=\frac{1}{p}\int_{\partial K}\left(\frac{\varphi_{L}}{\varphi_{K}}\right)^{p}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu=\frac{e^{pr_{L}}}{p}\int_{\partial K}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})^{p}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu

For abbreviation, let
r^{k}_{t}=I_{k}^{-1}((\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t}))
and
\chi(t)=e^{pr^{k}_{t}}
.
A similar calculation as in the proof of Proposition
10.1
gives

\displaystyle\chi^{\prime}(0)=

\displaystyle p\left(\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-kr^{k}_{K}}\right)^{-1}e^{pr^{k}_{K}}\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\widetilde{W}_{k}(\Omega_{t})

(10.58)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{pr_{L}}\left(\omega_{n}\sinh^{n-k}r^{k}_{K}e^{-kr^{k}_{K}}\right)^{-1}e^{pr^{k}_{K}}\int_{\partial K}(\cosh r-\tilde{u})^{p}p_{k}(\tilde{\kappa})d\mu\geq e^{pr_{L}},

where we used Theorem
10.10
in the last inequality. Equality holds in (
10.58
) if and only if
K=\Omega_{0}
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.
Instead of
K
, if we set
\Omega_{t_{0}}
as the initial uniformly h-convex bounded domain, then we can get
\chi^{\prime}(t_{0})\geq e^{pr_{L}}
by the same calculation as in (
10.58
).
Hence

(10.59)

\displaystyle\chi(t)\geq\chi(0)+te^{pr_{L}}=\chi(0)+t\exp\left(pI_{k}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(B(r_{L})))\right).

Thus (
10.56
) follows by the above inequality and the definition of
\chi(t)
in this proof.

By the equality case of (
10.58
), if
t>0
and equality holds in (
10.59
), then
K
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin. Conversely, if
K
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin, then for all
t\geq 0
, it is easy to see that equality holds in (
10.59
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
10.7
.
∎

Instead of using Theorem
10.10
, if we let
p=1
and use Theorem
10.11
in (
10.58
), then we can prove the following Proposition
10.8
by a similar argument as in Proposition
10.7
. Here we omit the proof.

Proposition 10.8
.

Conjecture
10.3
holds in the case
n\geq 1
,
k=0
,
p=1
,
a=1
,
b=t\geq 0
,
K
is a smooth uniformly h-convex bounded domain in
\mathbb{H}^{n+1}
which contains the origin in its interior and
L
is a geodesic ball of radius
r_{L}\geq 0
centered at the origin, i.e.

\exp\left(I_{0}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{0}(\Omega_{t}))\right)\geq\exp\left(I_{0}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{0}(K))\right)+t\exp\left(I_{0}^{-1}(\widetilde{W}_{k}(B(r_{L})))\right),

where
\Omega_{t}=K+_{1}t\cdot B(r_{L})
. If
t>0
, then equality holds if and only if
K
is a geodesic ball centered at the origin.

10.4.

In this subsection, we summarize the results of Conjecture

4.6

10.2

holds in one of the following cases,

and

,

,

,

and

is a geodesic ball centered at an interior point of

,

,
